# Vector Hands continuation checkpoint

**Lane state at HF-8 (16 November 2026 UTC), with HF-5's two Windows runs recorded.** The source head is `ef9f809d` at helper `1.0.15`; the HF-6, HF-7 and HF-8 commits are docs only. HF-5's runs at `d9d5afaf`, which has the `ef9f809d` sources, compiled it under MSVC and passed `apps-running-target` on x64 in both runs and on ARM64 in run 2. Run 1 never reached ARM64, so HF-5 stays awaiting CI until the lead rules on one ARM64-only dispatch and on the coverage change ([HF-5 runs](#hf-5-runs--windows-x64-and-arm64-at-the-hf-5-source-16-november-2026)). No measured macOS input file differs from `0ee486e3`, whose mac-build job `132574657008` passed the enforced `1.0.15` lock; macOS sources and the lock file are unchanged, and lock confirmation at the merged source is pending. PR #33 merged into `main` as `b362fdf6` ([HF-8](#hf-8--lane-state-and-ledgers-16-november-2026)), and `hands-finish` then merged `main` `2034e1da` in `c8ab4d87` ([merged main after #33](#merged-main-after-33-16-november-2026)).

The merged native-stop-fix diagnostics (HF-1) do two things:

- they keep the first UIA phase observed at a timeout immutable, for `stop-root-retired` / `core_failure:no_ui_tree` (`8e792a72`, with the sentinel fix in `503418a1`);
- they add 20 closed substages for the ARM64 failures (`a0fbfa3b`): apps-list request, reply, decode and target; 13 affinity-plan stages; and input-health context, target and ready.

They change no predicate, budget, retry or authority, they are not a functional fix or ARM64 qualification, and they establish no root cause. The merged source reports helper `1.0.15` with a new Windows-only source digest, which is not yet observed in CI. `1.0.16` and `1.0.17` in the diagnostic note are historical native-stop-fix labels. The exact `503418a1` x64 Stop pass is preserved at its own source.

- **Fixed, with evidence:** HF-4's prerequisite `289af6bb` fixed the folded native test that did not compile (C2660 in run 43638862293, job `132581736096`). Three Windows dispatches then built and ran at that head: `apps-running-target` failed at `apps-list-reply` / `paused` on x64 in two runs and on ARM64, the staged Stop failed once at `observe-up`, and one ARM64 required core check failed ([HF-4](#hf-4--folded-diagnostic-windows-x64-and-arm64-native-evidence-15-november-2026)).
- **Changed, awaiting CI:** HF-5 (`ef9f809d`) traced that `paused` reply to a Dispatcher pause cancellation of the in-flight `apps.list`, most likely at the first native-detection loss, the only verdict an uncalibrated self-test session can publish (a physical takeover is excluded by the HF-4 rows, which strongly supports but does not prove it; in the HF-5 runs a takeover would show as `physical_takeover` skips), and makes the isolated self-test settle that verdict before its first session-bound row. Its two Windows dispatches have not run ([HF-5](#hf-5--windows-apps-running-target-detection-pause-ordering-16-november-2026)).
- **Cause still unidentified:** the ARM64 affinity and listener rows ([HF-6](#hf-6--arm64-listener-health-and-affinity-rows-cause-still-unidentified-16-november-2026)); the `f830e3fd` `stop-root-retired` Stop, which has no phase sample, and the `observe-up` Stop at `289af6bb` ([HF-7](#hf-7--windows-staged-stop-stop-root-retired-not-reproduced-after-hf-1-no-phase-sample-16-november-2026)); HF-7 (1)'s first phase sample, `uia-root-element` / `core_failure:no_ui_tree` at merge `66164bf9` (job `133443621242`), for which closed lookup facts were added ([HF-7 (1) phase sample](#hf-7-1-phase-sample--uia-root-element-at-66164bf9-diagnostics-only-16-november-2026)); the x64 `apps-running-target` failures at `f830e3fd` and `0ee486e3`, which printed no stage; the `cf2373c4` x64 capture rows; and, for the lead, ARM64 `capture-color-patch` and the run 3 ARM64 core `unit` row ([current state](./hands-current-state.md#cause-still-unidentified)).
- **Pending CI:** HF-5's two dispatches, HF-2's Windows engine-shard line ([HF-2](#hf-2--mcp-and-pty-isolation-order-tests-15-november-2026)) and Mac lock confirmation at the merged source.

The integration retains both Hands and main's boundaries:

- Helper/subagent prompt inheritance (`session/fork.ts`) strips computer screenshots and desktop authority, including warm-provider request copies. This is distinct from a user-created session fork, which intentionally copies local history and follows screenshot deletion/retention rules. The bounded audit found no screenshot re-emission or durable restoration through the helper cache; it does not claim immediate zeroization of RAM retained until cache expiry.
- Terminal and background shell paths retain E10's fresh, one-use `#after-screen ` confirmation and recheck after asynchronous setup before input/spawn. A saved or Always answer cannot substitute, and a held Hands lease still prevents terminal interaction.
- Share/export/event integration preserves explicit screenshot opt-in, default stripping, prune tombstones and guest/forwarded-guest projection alongside main's audience fences and accounting projections.

The finer Windows Stop stages and the never-shipped fixture's early Chromium accessibility flag are **diagnostics, not a proven native fix**. Full merged regression, Node/Electron/Hands UI checks and the guarded artifacts-only packaging preview must record their actual source/run outcomes. Real-device grants, UAC/hardware/monitor checks, signing/notarization, Reset verification, Codium image-gateway deployment and owner publication approval remain separate gates. No owner OS permission or public release action is implied by this continuation.

A temporary mutation that removed the submit in-flight guard and the edited-draft check made exactly those two race tests fail. The app, ui and browser-fixture (`typecheck:e2e`) typechecks pass. A review pass then fixed: an Enter on an edited draft during the check now sends the newer draft once (previously both Enters were dropped); a send whose session changed during the check stops without sending or queueing there; a queued item refused at dispatch no longer marks a running session idle; readiness reads are bounded by 5 s and fail closed; probe-only blockers (Windows administrator, capture blocked, helper failure) show their own sentence; and the @Hands row shows the sentence of what setup cannot fix instead of "Off — set it up", opening no onboarding. Four new regressions (edited-draft re-Enter, session switch, and the two queued-dispatch status checks) failed against the code before these fixes. After them, each file run alone: submit 80, send-gate 14, mention 11 and mention-lookup 10 tests pass, and the app and browser-fixture typechecks pass. `hands-mention.spec.ts` gains two cases, a company-off row and a queued @Hands item refused at dispatch then sent from the dock, which run only in CI. A second review pass fixed: app lookups and readiness reads that changed nothing still rebuilt the @ list, and any rebuild moved the keyboard selection back to the first row, so Enter could insert the wrong item (the lookup now publishes only real changes, and the @ list keeps a row the user moved to through a new opt-in `keepActive` in `useFilteredList`); up to nine Computer rows could push every file result out of the ten visible rows (at most three apps now show while the query finds files); a message sent while a queued item was being checked at dispatch went out before it (the session page now queues behind a dispatching item); and a marked send on a draft tab kept going after a switch to another draft tab and promoted the wrong tab (the send now stops when the draft id changed and always promotes the tab it started on). Regressions: the lookup's no-change test, two draft-tab submit tests and two hook tests each failed against the code before the fix; the hook test runs with the browser condition from `test-browser/filtered-list-active.test.ts`. After the fixes, each file run alone: submit 82, mention-lookup 11, fake-platform 12 and filtered-list-active 4 tests pass, and the app, ui and browser-fixture typechecks pass. `hands-mention.spec.ts` gains two more CI-only cases: late app rows keep the files in view and the chosen row (the fake platform can now hold `apps()` answers), and a message sent during a queued item's dispatch check queues behind it. The two Windows failures of run 40813327379 did not recur: Windows unit job `123880822522` and Windows engine shard 6 job `123880822803` of 40999656844 passed. The earlier `windows-powershell` timeout was not reproduced in these fresh runs; its cause is not established. [Production benchmark 40999653908](https://github.com/scr1pter/claudeondfsfdaf/actions/runs/40999653908) passed all 12 measurements against [baseline 40987984437](https://github.com/scr1pter/claudeondfsfdaf/actions/runs/40987984437) at `50ac11c9`. Medians of three, read from each run's job log during D6b planning, baseline → D5b, in ms: task first/stable 148.1/164.3 → 126.9/152.6; new session 164.2/252.7 → 154.8/241.6; child session 106.0/175.2 → 90.1/130.4; streaming initial/completion 572.2/10398.8 → 555.5/10394.3. Both runs had zero blank or unknown samples, all 40 deltas with none pending, no replaced row or markdown and zero long tasks, and the scan found no fixture markers in 844 production JavaScript files. The hosted CPU model is recorded only in the artifact manifest, and the artifact host is refused by this environment's egress proxy, so no metrics files were committed and the deltas are observations only. If a later session can download artifact `12952514214` (D5b) and the baseline's, commit `d5b-manifest.json`, `d5b-metrics.json` and `d5b-comparison.json` in the D4 format. Run 40999653908 is the baseline for D6b.
- D5b open risks for later packages: an app-side quick-lane flag cannot keep "@Hands hi" off the tool-less quick lane, because the engine sizes requests itself and ignores markers, so this needs a core `TaskSize` mention input read by the engine and the app (D12 journey review). D22 must confirm the real preload delivers `prefs().policy.apps` as a `Map`, or `appOptions` must accept a plain object. Each marked send makes two helper permission probes on the real desktop, one at submit and one at dispatch. On the web build or the Linux desktop, a restored pill is refused with the platform sentence until the user removes it.
- D6b: the Settings › Hands page (`components/settings-v2/computer.tsx`) replaces D3's temporary `computer-status.tsx` panel, with its pure model `computer-model.ts` (`ComputerSettings`) and asynchronous controller `computer-controller.ts`. It has the status pill (company off, setup-unfixable blockers as Off with their sentence, Needs permissions, Preview, Ready, and a failed-read state), Use (on opens onboarding in place of Settings; off writes `enabled: false`), the onboarding permission rows with Open/Reset/Check again, While it works and When it needs the mouse (macOS), keep-awake, notifications, the stop shortcut with an inline [Change] recorder, the Always-allowed list with Remove and Remove all, company rules (read-only, with ignored rules), blocked apps (fixed built-in groups, defaults with Allow/Block, your blocks with Unblock and Block an app…), screenshot retention clamped to the company maximum, and Delete all with an inline confirmation. Company-locked controls are disabled and say "Set by your company". The Settings registry uses the `hands` icon. A root `ComputerMotionMirror` (`features/computer/motion-mirror.ts`) sends the Reduced motion setting to main. `ComputerPlatform.block` takes an `apps()` row `{key, name}`, and the dialog stack lets a key recorder take Escape (`packages/ui` dialog). Deviations D6b.1–13. Local evidence, each file run alone: computer-model 20, computer-controller 21, motion-mirror 6, settings-registry 15, fake-platform 13, onboarding-model 13, onboarding-controller 13, send-gate 14, mention-lookup 11, submit 82, security-policy 2, en 9 and brand-guard 5 tests passed. Temporarily removing the controller's snapshot guard or its Delete-all join made exactly the matching race test fail. The schema, app, ui and browser-fixture (`typecheck:e2e`) typechecks pass. Browser specs, CI only: the new `hands-settings.spec.ts` (search, Remove/Remove all, company locks and rules, retention cap, Delete all confirm, blocked apps, Use on/off, the reduced-motion mirror, stop shortcut recording including an Escape chord that keeps Settings open), `hands-smoke.spec.ts` rescripted for the new pill, and `hands-onboarding.spec.ts` opening the wizard from the Use switch. A review pass then fixed: a rejected While it works or When it needs the mouse save left the unsaved radio selected beside the error (the page now restores the group to main's value once the save settles; a value main answered differently was already republished and redrawn); a hung permission probe held the page in Loading and every queued change, Use off included (Settings' `permissions()` and `recheck()` now fail as a check after 5 s); a failed reduced-motion read or write was dropped until the setting changed (the mirror now retries after 1 s, 5 s and 30 s, and the app root disposes it); and the fake stored a `builtin:` key passed to `block()`, which main will refuse (it now ignores keys `parseAppKey` rejects). Temporary reproductions and mutations: a happy-dom render of the real page showed the rejected-pointer radio stay selected before the fix and pass after it (not committed; it stubbed the UI kit); removing the probe limit or the mirror's retry made exactly the new controller test and the two new mirror tests fail. After the fixes, each file run alone: computer-controller 22, motion-mirror 8 and fake-platform 13 tests pass. Final gate before commit, after the last source edit and each run alone: computer-controller 22, motion-mirror 8 and fake-platform 13 tests passed again, and the app (`tsgo -b`) and browser-fixture (`typecheck:e2e`) typechecks passed; the push hook then typechecks every package. `hands-settings.spec.ts` gains a CI-only case: a mode change main keeps as Take the screen, and a rejected mouse or mode save, leave the saved radio selected. **Pending CI:** the focused `hands_ui` run (Linux and Windows), the production benchmark compared with baseline 40999653908 (app.tsx gains a root mirror; the web build does nothing), and fresh full CI.
- D6b open risks for later packages: D15/D22 must accept `block({key, name})` (validate with `parseAppKey`, clean the name, store `{key, name, addedAt}`) and `setPrefs({unblocked})` restricted to DEFAULT_BLOCKED keys, and must deliver `policy.apps` as a `Map` or company rules show nothing. D23 still owns cache invalidation after Delete all and retention scheduling. D20 owns chord registration and refusals; the renderer refuses only the obvious OS chords. On the real desktop the mirror adds one `prefs()` read per launch, and up to three retries 1 s, 5 s and 30 s apart only when a read or write fails. The docs link `/docs/vector-hands` resolves only after C9.

- D6b follow-up fix (`components/settings-v2/computer.tsx`): the retention `SelectV2` is now `modal`. The Settings dialog is a modal Kobalte dialog; Kobalte 0.13.11's `ariaHideOutside` keeps observing `body` and marks any later-added node aria-hidden unless another modal layer takes over. The select is non-modal by default and portals its listbox into `body`, so its options were hidden from assistive technology and from Playwright's role queries, while the dialog's modal dropdown menus, which start their own hide-outside, were not. This is inferred from the Kobalte source and the spec's symptoms (the failure's error context could not be downloaded); only a new `hands_ui` run can confirm it. The spec's role-based assertions are unchanged. The Settings › General `SelectV2` has the same latent problem and no test; it is outside Hands and not changed here. Local evidence: the app (`tsgo -b`) and browser-fixture (`typecheck:e2e`) typechecks pass.
- D7: `dialog-share-session.tsx` adds "Include screenshots" (off in every dialog, with `computer.share.help`) for transcripts with a completed Hands step. Ticking or unticking reloads the review with the matching `includeScreenshots` query, clears the acknowledgement, and keeps the box and Refresh disabled until the newest review's hash is stored; publish sends `includeScreenshots` for exactly that review, and the review text lists each screenshot. An opted-in review over 4 MB cannot be acknowledged and says to untick; the share-preview routes now use `PublicSessionShare.review`, which refuses such a review with `TOO_LARGE` instead of sending every screenshot (local `vector export` is not capped). The automatic-sharing consent shows "Auto-shares never include screenshots." The public viewer (`packages/web/src/components/Share.tsx`) shows opted-in screenshots as embedded images inside each computer step, with a count in its summary and a header sentence; its revocation check no longer rebuilds an unchanged revision, so an opened step keeps its images. Core publication (`public-session-share.ts`): a manual publish withdraws an unconfirmed earlier attempt (marking the row `deleting` before the removal request and deleting it only after the service confirms, so a lost answer never leaves a row the live-update queue re-creates; the next publish resumes the removal), so a later text-only request never resends that attempt's archive or screenshots; an opted-in publication must match its review hash even with future updates; and a reviewed publication (one with a `previewHash` or `includeScreenshots: true`) that finds a confirmed share answers `CONFLICT` instead of reporting that share as its own, while unreviewed callers such as `vector run --share` keep receiving the existing share. The live-update queue no longer completes an unconfirmed opted-in attempt in the background; it withdraws it (D7.10), while text-only attempts keep E13's background completion. A reviewed request that finds a confirmed share republishes that share to the session before answering `CONFLICT`, so the refreshed dialog shows it with Unshare even when a stale session update had cleared it. Deviations D7.1–10. Local evidence before the review fixes below, each file run alone: `app/src/components/dialog-share-session.test.tsx` 8 (new; happy-dom with the Babel Solid transform and a pass-through dialog frame), `core/test/public-session-share.test.ts` 32 (run with fresh XDG roots; new regressions for the withdrawal including a failed DELETE and a DELETE applied with its answer lost, a share confirmed meanwhile, unreviewed publications of a shared or auto-shared session, the review hash with future updates, and the review size cap), `web/test/public-share-viewer.test.ts` 5 (one new test) and `app/src/features/computer/brand-guard.test.ts` 5 tests passed. Temporary mutations, each restored: publishing without the flag failed all 7 original dialog tests; not reloading on toggle failed 4; re-enabling the controls before the digest (the old timing) failed 5, including the digest-window assertion; restoring E13's reuse of the earlier attempt failed the withdrawal regression; and removing the confirmed-share conflict, the hash check for opted-in publications with updates, the review cap, the withdrawal's remote-first order, or the dialog's mapping of the engine refusal each failed exactly its matching new test. A review fix then limited that conflict to reviewed requests (applying it to every request failed only the new unreviewed-publication test, with the CLI's abort message) and stored the withdrawal intent before the request (restoring the old order failed both withdrawal tests, and without the state assertion the lost-answer test saw the queue re-POST the withdrawn attempt; routing a `deleting` row back to "Retry Unshare" failed both withdrawal tests). The viewer's same-revision fix has no local component test; a scratch check of the Solid store showed that the old same-revision replacement re-ran the keyed `Show` while the merge keeps the snapshot and still updates its details, and `public-share-viewer.spec.ts` gains the browser case. The core, engine, app (`tsgo -b`), browser-fixture (`typecheck:e2e`) and web (`astro check`, 0 errors) typechecks pass. Browser specs, CI only (full CI's e2e job, Linux and Windows): `public-share-consent.spec.ts` now checks the unticked box, its help text, previews without the flag and `includeScreenshots: false` in both layouts, plus a new opt-in case (reloaded review with the screenshot line, cleared acknowledgement, the auto-share sentence, and `includeScreenshots: true` with the screenshot review's hash); `public-share-viewer.spec.ts` (through `test:e2e:viewer`) gains embedded rendering with no outside requests, a text-only shell step, no horizontal scroll at 390 px, an opened step that stays open with the same image across a same-revision check whose new update time is shown, and removal on a revision without screenshots. `packages/web/test/public-share-viewer.test.ts` runs in no CI lane (the web package has no test script and the unit job runs only `release-copy.test.ts`), so the local run is its only evidence. D7 changes no session or timeline code; the dialog is loaded on demand. **Pending CI:** `hands_ui` for the D6b follow-up fix, and one fresh full CI run for D6b and D7.
- D7 review fixes: the dialog shows the untick advice only while the newest screenshot-free review fits the limit, and otherwise the engine's own message (D7.4); the live-update queue withdraws an unconfirmed opted-in attempt instead of publishing its screenshots later (D7.10); the reviewed-publication conflict restates the stored share first (D7.9); and two cross-instance regressions cover the withdrawal's guarded mark (D7.6). New regressions: the dialog's "text alone over the limit" case (both an engine refusal and an over-limit review), the queue withdrawal for a refused create and for one whose acknowledgement was lost, a background-completed text-only attempt whose cleared session share is restored by the conflict, a withdrawal by another instance while this instance's create is in flight, and a confirmation by another instance between a publication's read and its mark. Temporary mutations, each restored: dropping the dialog's text-size guard failed only the new dialog test; dropping the mark's state and revision guard failed only the read-then-confirm test; letting the queue complete opted-in attempts failed only the queue withdrawal test; dropping the conflict's restatement failed only the confirmed-meanwhile test; and sending the withdrawal's DELETE to another id failed all four withdrawal tests. After the fixes, each file run alone: `dialog-share-session.test.tsx` 9 and `core/test/public-session-share.test.ts` 35 (fresh XDG roots) tests pass, and the core (`tsgo --noEmit`) and app (`tsgo -b`) typechecks pass.
- D7 final gate, rerun on the committed tree after the last source edit, each through the lock wrapper and one at a time: `packages/app` `src/components/dialog-share-session.test.tsx` 9 passed, 0 failed (108 expects); `packages/core` `test/public-session-share.test.ts` 35 passed, 0 failed (261 expects, fresh XDG roots and `VECTOR_TEST_HOME`); `packages/web` `test/public-share-viewer.test.ts` 5 passed, 0 failed (32 expects). Typechecks: core (`tsgo --noEmit`), app (`tsgo -b`), browser fixtures (`typecheck:e2e`) and web (`astro check`: 0 errors, 0 warnings, 96 hints in unrelated files) all exit 0; the engine and every other package are typechecked by the pre-push hook. After the push, one focused `hands_ui` run (the `hands-*.spec.ts` browser specs, which cover the D6b follow-up fix) and one `hands_benchmark` run (D7's after-run; baseline D6b run 41007831957) are dispatched; their results are recorded with the next package. The D7 browser specs (`public-share-consent.spec.ts`, `public-share-viewer.spec.ts`) run only in full CI's e2e job, which the coordinator dispatches per phase.
- D7 open risks: the site and API must deploy from a commit that contains E13 before a desktop with the E13 engine ships, because the share API on `main` decodes requests with `onExcessProperty: "error"` and knows neither `includeScreenshots` nor `screenshots`, so it would refuse every share from such a desktop, and `main`'s viewer would refuse snapshots with screenshots (C7/C9 owner step). The viewer relies on `/s/*` allowing `data:` images; a future Content-Security-Policy there must allow `img-src data:`. With "Include future updates" ticked, the screenshots disappear at the first real transcript update (E13.7), as `computer.share.description` says; because an opted-in publication now needs a matching review hash, a Hands run that is still adding steps can make publication ask for a fresh review. The share-preview cap still loads the whole archive in the engine to measure it. A Hands step with many screenshots can approach the 4 MB response limit; images stay inside closed steps with lazy decoding, but opening many steps on a phone costs memory. The viewer's 15-second revocation check (which predates D7) downloads the whole snapshot each time, and its same-revision merge then discards it; a near-limit screenshot share costs each open, visible tab roughly 4 MB per check (about 1 GB an hour). A conditional or metadata-only read needs a share API change (`api/_lib/public-shares.ts`, for example an ETag keyed on the share's revision and dates with a 304) and an owner SQL change (`docs/vector/owner-actions/sql/public-shares.sql`, a read that skips the archive), and the share API tests run in no CI lane; this is recorded for C9's owner steps, to land before the site deploys screenshot sharing.

D8.15 replaces the fix. These are back in the D5b range (40999653908: 126.9/152.6, 154.8/241.6, 90.1/130.4, 555.5/10394.3) and D7 changed no session or timeline code, so this is read as runner variance, not a regression; the CPU model is only in the artifact manifest (artifact `12995096747`), which this environment cannot download. Run 41093600950 is the baseline for D8. Fresh full CI for D6b and D7 has still not run.
- D8 (timeline run card), committed and pushed in `4b9fd1fe` on top of `61708d86`. Consecutive `computer` parts and the text and reasoning between them render as one collapsible card per run (`session-ui` `computer-tool.tsx`, `computer-labels.ts`, `message-part-group.ts`; app `message-timeline.tsx`): header "{{name}} · apps · N actions · duration", collapsed latest thumbnail (or "Screenshot removed") and last step, expanded Vector-built step labels with "The agent: …" notes only under steps, per-step View screenshot, and a preview with a violet ring at the clicked frame pixel. While a turn works its run keeps its trailing notes, so a live run grows in one row (D8.2). The engine now writes the 60-character `text.preview` (D8.8). The retention select fix of D8.15 rides along. Deviations D8.1–15. Local evidence, each file run alone through the lock wrapper: `session-ui` `computer-labels.test.ts` 19 and `message-part.test.ts` 19 (13 new grouping tests, 5 of them characterization tests that also passed against a copy of the pre-D8 function), app `projection.test.ts` 12, `main-copy.test.ts` 30 and `brand-guard.test.ts` 5, and `engine` `test/tool/computer.test.ts` 143 (fresh XDG roots and `VECTOR_TEST_HOME`) passed. Temporary mutations, each restored: a label that appends `metadata.intent` failed only "metadata.intent never appears in a label"; dropping the label's credential check failed only the typed-text cut test; counting paused parts failed only the two count tests; not absorbing notes failed the five absorption tests and none of the characterization tests; ignoring `open` failed only the two live-run tests; and an engine preview that is not cleaned or cut failed only the new engine regression. Typechecks: session-ui, ui, core, engine (`tsgo --noEmit`), app (`tsgo -b`) and browser fixtures (`typecheck:e2e`) pass. CI only: the new `session-timeline-tool-projection.spec.ts` cases (one card with "5 actions", labels and notes, removed marker, thumbnail and ring with a zoom crop, a `read` splitting two cards, an interrupted call labeled without its raw error, and a streamed run that keeps one card element and mounts no note row) run in full CI's e2e job, not in the `hands_ui` glob; the retention fix runs in `hands_ui`. The `hands_ui` and `hands_benchmark` results for `4b9fd1fe` are in the D8 CI item below; fresh full CI for D6b, D7 and D8 is still pending.
- D8 review fixes (committed in `4b9fd1fe`): (1) the engine wrote `text.preview` before the secure-field refusal, so text typed at a password field stayed in the part's metadata after the credential handoff. Text aimed at a credential field now gets no preview, and a handoff keeps only the length, which also covers a `secure_field`, `elevated` or `protected` refusal from the desktop at the point of input (D8.8). `computerLabel` now returns the recorded block, setup or handoff label, so the lone-part renderer never labels a handed-off call "Typed …" (D8.5). (2) The ring rule moved into `computerRing(part)` with unit tests, and the thumbnail spec gains a batch part whose point lies inside its frame (D8.12). (3) While the turn waits on a question, which renders nothing until answered, `runOpen` closes the live run, so the agent's words before the question are their own row instead of hiding in the collapsed card (D8.2). Reproduced before fixing: the strengthened secure-field test and the three new point-of-input tests failed with the plaintext preview. Temporary mutations, each restored: computing the preview for credential fields again failed only "text for a secure field keeps no preview when another refusal comes before the handoff"; dropping the handoff's preview strip failed only the three point-of-input tests; dropping `computerRing`'s batch guard failed only "only a single call's point rings its screenshot"; ignoring a recorded handoff in `computerLabel` failed four label tests; and `runOpen` ignoring questions failed only the new question test. After the fixes, each file run alone through the lock wrapper: `engine` `test/tool/computer.test.ts` 147 passed (fresh XDG roots and `VECTOR_TEST_HOME`), `session-ui` `computer-labels.test.ts` 21 and `message-part.test.ts` 20, and app `projection.test.ts` 12 passed. Typechecks: session-ui and engine (`tsgo --noEmit`), app (`tsgo -b`) and browser fixtures (`typecheck:e2e`) pass. CI only: the new spec case "a question the turn waits on ends a live Hands run before the agent's words" and the batch part of the ring case. `rows.ts` changed again, so the pending `hands_benchmark` after-run must run on the fixed tree.
- D8 second review fixes (committed in `4b9fd1fe`): (1) a stopped or paused call's action step was marked done with its typed text, so a Stop at a typing confirmation read "Typed “…” in Mail" then "Stopped" while the header counted 0; such a step is now `unconfirmed` with "May not have run" and no typed preview, the same rule the header uses (D8.9). (2) The collapsed card's last step now shows the same "Failed" or "May not have run" marker and weak colour as the expanded list (D8.5); the marker's colour used an undefined token and now uses `--icon-critical-base`. (3) A turn whose last text is a note inside a run card lost its Copy button, details and reply footer; the copy target is now the last text with its own row (`copyPartID`, `runPartIDs`; the timeline reads held IDs from its projected rows, D8.2). (4) In the engine, a `secure_field`, `elevated` or `protected` refusal whose record failed skipped the handoff and kept the typed preview; the preview is now dropped before recording (D8.8). Reproduced before fixing: the two new step-state tests and the three new engine tests ("typed text refused at input with <code> keeps no preview if its record fails") failed. Temporary mutations, each restored: showing the preview for unconfirmed calls failed only the stopped/paused test; marking paused calls done failed three step tests; letting `copyPartID` ignore run cards failed both copy tests. After the fixes, each file run alone through the lock wrapper: `engine` `test/tool/computer.test.ts` 150 passed (fresh XDG roots and `VECTOR_TEST_HOME`), `session-ui` `computer-labels.test.ts` 24 and `message-part.test.ts` 22, app `projection.test.ts` 12 and `main-copy.test.ts` 30 passed. Typechecks: session-ui, ui and engine (`tsgo --noEmit`), app (`tsgo -b`) and browser fixtures (`typecheck:e2e`) pass. CI only: the abort case now asserts the collapsed failure marker, and the new spec case "a stopped Hands call never reads as done, and the turn keeps its footer". `message-timeline.tsx` and `projection.ts` changed again, so the pending `hands_benchmark` after-run must run on this tree.
- D8 third review fixes (committed in `4b9fd1fe`): (1) the preview ring was drawn for a paused or handed-off click, whose screenshot is the fresh frame captured after the user while its point belonged to the frame before, so it could mark a spot the call never clicked; `computerRing` now refuses any call the header does not count, and the engine deletes `metadata.point` when a take-over or handoff begins (D8.12). (2) A batch's expanded card showed only "Clicked in Mail +2 more steps"; every step now has its own target-less label, marked as the header counts the call, before its block or handoff (D8.5, INV-T5). (3) A call refused before its input (a declined sensitive confirmation, for example) still read "Typed “…” in Bank"; the typed preview now shows only for a call the header counts (D8.9). (4) A turn whose only text was a note inside a run card lost its copy button, details line and Learned chip; the card holding the last note now draws them through the new shared `ReplyActions` (D8.2). Reproduced before fixing: the three new `computer-labels.test.ts` tests ("a refused call never shows the text it proposed as typed", "a batch lists each of its steps, marked as the header counts the call", "a click the header does not count has no ring") failed, and with the two engine deletions removed the three new engine tests ("a coordinate click with a paused wait / with a pointer wait / handed off keeps no point for the frame after the user") failed while their control ("a coordinate click keeps its point on an unchanged frame") passed. After the fixes, each file run alone through the lock wrapper: `engine` `test/tool/computer.test.ts` 154 passed (fresh XDG roots and `VECTOR_TEST_HOME`), `session-ui` `computer-labels.test.ts` 27 and `message-part.test.ts` 22, and app `projection.test.ts` 12 passed. - D9 open risks: until D22 provides `platform.computer`, the real desktop uses the engine event and keeps every renderer notification. A queued card on the engine path depends on the holder's events reaching this page's server and on the queued conversation's own status being known to the page; a holder in an unloaded directory that was busy before the page loaded shows only from its next event (at most about 2 s while it holds the computer). The final Done or Stopped state can be lost to the engine's throttle. Elapsed time uses the holder's clock. A card reply and a dock reply to the same app ask make the second fail with a toast. On the engine path Stop aborts the turn rather than halting input first. The card's action count is the desktop's `holder.actions`, while the timeline header uses D8's conservative count (D8.9). "Using <app>" relies on D18 keeping the current app last.

CI only: the new `e2e/regression/hands-plugins.spec.ts` (six cases: the rename, Community badge and Hands blurb with no notice; an installed copy's notice, Dismiss, and still dismissed after reopening and reloading; Hands off hiding the entry, its notice and its connected count while Chrome DevTools and Playwright stay; a Windows app rule hiding it without the desktop; unrelated settings keeping it; and the neutral blurb with no notice without Hands) runs in the `hands_ui` dispatch on Linux and Windows.
- D11 review fixes (in the same commit): (1) several of the browser spec's absence and count checks could pass before the state they check had loaded, because `sync().data.mcp` is `{}` and the catalog is whole until `GET /mcp` and the directory's `GET /config` answer, and Playwright stops retrying as soon as a condition first holds. The reload, reopen and without-Hands cases now first wait for the row's Remove button, which renders only for an installed server; the Hands-off case also installs and connects Chrome DevTools, waits for its Remove button and expects "1 connected", which a count of the whole catalog would read as 2; `openPlugins` waits for the `/config` response to finish before opening the dialog, so "unrelated company settings keep the entry" is checked after that config arrived. (2) The dialog's `dismissed: noticeReady() ? notice.dismissed : undefined` was checked only by inspection, since the web build's storage is synchronous. The new `src/components/dialog-select-plugins.test.tsx` renders the real dialog and catalog on a desktop-shaped platform whose storage reads answer only when the test releases them (only app contexts, the dialog frame and the switch are replaced; the dismissal goes through the real `persisted`): a stored dismissal never shows the notice before or after the read, and with none stored the notice waits for the read, then shows until Dismiss, which is written to `vector.global.dat`. Mutation, restored: `dismissed: notice.dismissed` failed both new tests.

- D12 step 0, the benchmark-watch audit of D8 and D9's per-delta work (read-only; no code changed). A temporary, uncommitted probe under `test-browser/` (run alone through the lock wrapper with `--conditions=browser --preload ./happydom.ts`, then deleted) built the real `createTimelineProjection` over a Solid store with 10 and with 80 turns (every fourth a Hands run with a note) and applied 40 text deltas the way the reducer's `message.part.delta` writes the part's text. `message-part.tsx` imports a Vite worker URL Bun cannot load, so the probe replaced `@vectordevai/session-ui/message-part` with the pure `message-part-group` module plus an equivalent `renderable` for its text and computer parts. Result, identical at 10 and 80 turns: 120 part reads over 40 deltas (3 per delta, from the active turn's two messages only: `constructMessageRows`' part refs and `runOpen`), and 0 recomputations of `rows`, `runParts` and `rowByKey` (row reuse keeps the same rows). By reading: the run card's and timeline's per-turn memos (`copyPartID`, `runPartIDs`, `ReplyActions`, the card's `tools`/`last`/`latest`) read only their own turn's parts and `runParts()`, which does not recompute on a delta; the composer's working line and the state provider's `listen` filter do constant work per event. No per-delta D8 or D9 cost that grows with history was found in the projection; component rendering under deltas was not measured locally, so the streaming-completion watch stays open. D12 changes no session or timeline code. Its one production change is the computer tool's batch-failure metadata (D12.8), which the benchmark never reaches. So its `hands_benchmark` after-run (baseline 41148101352) is a further control sample: if completion keeps rising with no session or timeline change, suspect the runner or the benchmark, not D8/D9.
- D12 (end-to-end journey), on top of `bcbbf885`. The CI-only `e2e/regression/hands-journey.spec.ts` walks 1.5 against the fake platform and a scripted engine on the page's event stream (`installSseTransport`), with a server whose message, permission, question and status reads follow the events, and every stream opens with `server.connected` as the real servers' do, so a stream the page's watchdog replaces makes it re-read them instead of losing what the dead stream dropped (D12.4). Four tests (D12.2): the first run (onboarding from the @Hands row with the clock held so only Check again moves the grants, consent then Finish re-sending the same `consentedAt`, examples disabled on the composer path, Settings' Ready from a pushed `ready` phase and an empty Always-allowed list; the marked send with exactly the two documented markers; the app ask armed at 700 ms on the page clock and answered once; working with "Using Numbers", the Tasks card and one run card that grows in place with its note; a click-only sensitive "Send" denied; take-over and Resume; the model's handoff answered with Continue in the question dock; Done with the card's desktop count and the header's "Numbers · 4 actions", the exact step labels and markers, and Settings still empty; the fake's whole call log), the second run (seeded with an earlier Hands turn: Always allow armed and exact, the grant recorded by the fake's new `grantAlways` as desktop main would, Stop on the card sending only `command("stop")` and no abort, the stopped call and aborted turn ending in Stopped with the Interrupted marker after the card, and Settings listing Numbers "From Apple Inc." with Remove), screen-reader mode (a refused coordinate click with the engine's real metadata, no `app`, reading "Clicked in the app" marked Failed beside `read_ui` and a ref click, "Numbers · 3 actions", and no refusal text), and a dropped stream (a turn's prompt, step, busy status and app ask reach only the server's reads, the watchdog fires on the page clock, and the prompt, the ask and then the working line come back from the re-reads, the ask answered once and the turn going on over the new stream). `FakeComputer.grantAlways` (D12.1) and the matching `e2e/utils/fake-computer.ts` helper are fixture-only; `e2e/tsconfig.json` lists the spec. Deviations D12.1–8, which also correct the plan: the handoff uses the engine's real `other` reason, so the card reads "Your turn in Numbers: finish this step yourself" (D12.5), and D8.9's executed count moves to D19 and D22 (D12.6). The review of D12 led to three changes. First, every live and seeded turn now has the engine's shape: one assistant message per provider step, so the run card, its notes and the Interrupted marker are asserted across message boundaries (D12.7). Second, stream writes retry only the transport's "no active connection" error, which the page's 15 s watchdog can cause between the open-stream check and the write. Third, an E6b fix: a call suppressed with `BATCH_FAILURE` now carries `metadata.code = "failed"`, so the run card no longer shows it as done or counts it (D12.8). A second review found that the write retry covered only a stream that was already gone: no journey stream sent `server.connected`, the page's only resync signal, so an event a dying stream had taken but not handed over was lost and nothing re-read it. `installSseTransport` gained an opt-in `connected` option that opens every stream with that event (other callers are unchanged), the journey turns it on, and the fourth test pins the resync (D12.4); after it, `typecheck:e2e` exits 0 (a temporary malformed ask in the fourth test failed it with TS2739, restored) and `./e2e/performance/timeline-stability/fixture.test.ts`, which imports the transport, passed 4 of 4. Local evidence, each through the lock wrapper from `packages/app`: `src/features/computer/fake-platform.test.ts` 14 passed (80 expects; the new grant test included) and `src/features/computer/brand-guard.test.ts` 5 passed (25 expects), 0 failed in each; a temporary mutation keeping duplicate keys in `grantAlways` failed only the new test (13 passed, 1 failed), restored. From `packages/engine`, with fresh XDG roots: the strengthened `test/tool/computer.test.ts` case failed before the D12.8 fix (the metadata had no `code`), and after it the whole file passed (154 passed, 0 failed, 930 expects). Typechecks: app (`tsgo -b`), browser fixtures (`typecheck:e2e`, rerun after each review's changes) and engine (`tsgo --noEmit`) exit 0. Prettier is clean on every touched source, test, spec and config file. Not run locally by rule: the new spec, which runs only in CI. Final gate before the D12 commit, after the spec's last edit, each through the lock wrapper from `packages/app`: `typecheck:e2e` exit 0, `./e2e/performance/timeline-stability/fixture.test.ts` 4 passed (7 expects), `src/features/computer/fake-platform.test.ts` 14 passed (80 expects), 0 failed in each, and Prettier clean on every touched source, test, spec and config file; the engine file and the app sources had not changed since they last passed, and the pre-push hook typechecks every package. - C1 (shared helper inputs and the fake helper; deviations C1.1 to C1.12). `native/computer-helper/identity.json` (section 2.11), the generated and committed `blocklist.json` (49,548 bytes: every `TABLE` entry in order with its list, patterns and `removable` on the two blocking lists, the category rules, the `enforce` and `click-only` rules and the never reasons) and `README.md`; `scripts/computer-helper-identity.ts` (`generateBlocklist`, `generateIdentitySources(outDir)` rendering `Identity.swift`, `identity.h` and `blocklist.h` as build outputs, refusing a stale `blocklist.json`) and `scripts/channel-ids.ts` (electron-builder's `APP_IDS` and `EXECUTABLE_NAMES`, moved unchanged); ten conformance transcripts in `test/fixtures/computer-protocol/` with the shared framing and replay driver `test/computer/transcript.ts`, which decodes every frame against the shared schemas before matching; and the fake helper (`test/computer/fake-helper.ts`, `fake-desktop.ts`, `png.ts`): a virtual desktop with Calculator, Notes and its locked-note password field, Terminal, Vector and its overlay, a System Settings privacy pane, a fictional bank app, the desktop furniture and a notification toast on one or two displays, PNG frames that paint every unapproved and never-listed window black and Vector as one block with the overlay left out, hit-tests and a UI tree from the same model, atomic input with the C1.6 check order, the 5 s heartbeat halt, stop, user input, secure input, lock and display-change events, a manual clock, in-process `createFakeHelper` and `connect`, and the `--version`, `--probe` and `--stdio` command line. Mutations, each restored by copying the file back from `<scratch>/vector-C1/mutations`: a hand-edited Terminal id in `blocklist.json` failed the committed-equals-generated test (plus the two generated-source tests, which refuse or depend on the stale file) and the fake's agreement test; a non-removable Disk Utility in the generator failed the drift, removable and generated-source tests; `stop` sent before the release failed only the Escape test; an unapproved window left unpainted failed the four capture and approval tests that look at it; a skipped heartbeat halt failed the heartbeat-loss transcript and the two heartbeat tests; a halt latched at `session.begin` failed 16 tests, the lower-bound heartbeat test among them; failure frames without `error` failed the five transcripts with a failure and the two cancel tests through the driver's decoding. Review fixes before the commit (recorded in C1.1 to C1.4, C1.6, C1.8 and C1.9): `.gitattributes` keeps `native/computer-helper/*.json` and the transcripts LF, because a simulated `core.autocrlf=true` checkout wrote `blocklist.json` with CRLF on all 2,214 lines (none after the entry), which the committed-equals-generated test and `--out` refuse; `fit` returns the one scale both sides were cut with and the fake reports it as `imageScale`, because the smaller side ratio left a 1000 by 640 display's last column unpainted under `maxWidth: 999` and a hit-test there answered `stale_frame` (reproduced, now painted and hit); the generator requires `displayName` to equal `Hands.NAME` exactly, which `"Vector"` passed before; helpers refuse text input into a click-only (developer) app with `blocked` (a `click-only` rule in `blocklist.json`, Visual Studio Code in the virtual desktop), and a key op that types (no modifier but Shift or AltGraph, a character key) meets the same secure-field and click-only floors as `input.type`; the replay driver skips and returns events a line does not expect, so a helper's `input_detection` on a runner without grants no longer fails `session.jsonl` or `heartbeat-loss.jsonl`; and the generator's header and C1.1 now say it runs under Bun only (plain Node stopped with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` on `./channel-ids`). - D13 (the bridge server; deviations D13.1 to D13.14), implemented on top of `0ebc4203` and committed as `feat(hands): D13 add the bridge server`. `src/main/computer-bridge-input.ts`: `parseBridgeRequest` (UTF-8 checked with `isUtf8`, a byte-order mark refused, the E0 `BridgeRequest` decoded with no unknown field at any depth) and `parseProtocol`. `src/main/computer-bridge.ts`: `ComputerError`, the `ComputerExecutor` seam with its frozen per-request key, `unavailableExecutor` (D19 replaces it), `createBridgeHandler` and `startComputerBridge({executor, capabilities}) -> {url, token, stop()}` on 127.0.0.1 with a `randomBytes(32)` hex token. Host and token come first (401; `timingSafeEqual` over SHA-256 digests; `Host`, `Authorization` and the protocol header each sent exactly once), then 404, 426, 413 (a declared length at once, a streamed body as it passes 64 KiB, with nothing past it kept), 400, and 409 `{ok: false, code, error}` only for the executor's refusals; refusals close the connection, so an unread body is never drained. Results are encoded with their command's response schema (unknown fields and explicit undefined optional keys dropped, a mismatch 409 failed, over 8 MiB 409 `frame_too_large`, exactly 8 MiB sent); other throws are 409 failed with a fixed sentence and logged by command and error name only; a `ComputerError`'s text has the token redacted and fits 2000 UTF-16 units. A client that leaves (the response's or its connection's close before the response finished) cancels its pending run once and never a settled one, and one that leaves before its command starts never starts it; a lease its client never received is released on the acquire's envelope; `stop()` cancels and answers open requests 409 unavailable, refuses new work and closes within 1 s. `test/computer/bridge-node.ts` checks the bundled bridge under Node and is in the desktop tsconfig; test.yml's desktop computer-use step also runs `./src/main/computer-*.test.ts` and then bundles the bridge and runs that check under the job's Node 24. The plan's critique was checked against the code and all ten items were taken: encoding instead of decoding (a probe showed `decodeUnknownOption` refusing `{done: 1, frame: undefined}` and `encodeUnknownOption` accepting it with the key dropped), the leave-before-run window, both undelivered-lease windows (an answer cut off before it finished, and a lease arriving after `stop()`), `isUtf8` instead of a throwing fatal `TextDecoder`, the UTF-16 cut (2000 emoji were 4000 units after `Computer.clean` and failed `BridgeFailure`), `connection: close` on refusals, the C8 gap and the D19 cancel rules (D13.12 to D13.14), the extra race tests, and a CI note: C1's unit-lane step has since passed on both OSes at `0ebc4203` (41180504513), so the full run on D13's commit validates D13's additions; the step prints each file and the Node check on its own line, so each result stays attributable. Local evidence, each Bun workload through the lock wrapper from `packages/desktop` (and `packages/app` for the guard) with `TMPDIR=<scratch>/vector-D13` and fresh XDG roots and VECTOR_TEST_HOME under `<scratch>/vector-D13/gate-final`: `src/main/computer-bridge.test.ts` 33 passed, 0 failed (256 expects), in-process (real bridges on 127.0.0.1 with `fetch` and raw `node:net` requests, plus the handler seam; no subprocess, Electron, helper or OS access); `bun build ./src/main/computer-bridge.ts --target=node --format=esm` (0.89 MB, only `node:` imports) then `node ./test/computer/bridge-node.ts` under Node 22.22.0: 7 of 7 checks passed; `packages/app` `src/features/computer/brand-guard.test.ts` 5 passed; `bun typecheck` (`tsgo -b`) exits 0, and a temporary type error appended to `computer-bridge-input.ts` and to `bridge-node.ts` made it fail (restored), so both are covered; Prettier clean on every touched source, test, config and workflow file and on the new deviation lines. Runtime probes (scratch in `<scratch>/vector-D13`): Bun 1.3.14's `node:http` reports no client disconnect and fires the response's close only after a finished answer; under Node 20 of 20 clients that sent a whole act and left at once had it started and then cancelled. Mutations, each restored by copying the file back from `<scratch>/vector-D13/mutations`, each failing only its own tests: no Host check (the Host test and the duplicate-Host test); `===` for the token (the `timingSafeEqual` test); cancel on the request's close (four Node checks, the normally answered one among them; under Bun such requests are never answered, so that run was ended after its first failures); no undelivered-lease release (the four lease tests and the Node lease check); the result sent unencoded, and decoding instead of encoding (the stripping and undefined-key test); `>=` at 64 KiB (the boundary test); a plain Error's text let through (the fixed-text test); refusals without `connection: close` (the refused-body seam test, the Bun 413 test, and the Node 413 and 401 checks); a run started after its client left (the leave-before-run test); no UTF-16 cut (the astral-text test); cancel without its once guard (the stop-and-leave test); cancel after the run settled (the cut-off-lease test); `stop()` without cancel (both stop tests and the Node stop check); the token not redacted (the redaction test). Pending, CI only: the unit step on Linux and Windows (the Bun test in its own process and the Node 24 check, including whether a Windows client still reads the early 413 and the 401 answers: the bridge closes both connections while the client may still be sending, and on Windows a reset that arrives before the client reads an answer can erase it; if CI shows that, refusals get a bounded lingering close in the bridge, keeping the no-endless-drain property and the check as it is), which needs a dispatched full run; the real engine transport (E6a) and probe (E5) against this bridge only meet once D22 starts it; Electron's own Node runtime for the disconnect and stop paths is a recorded gap (D13.14). D13 changes no renderer, session or timeline code, so it needs no `hands_ui` or `hands_benchmark` run.
- D13 review fixes (in the same commit): (1) and (2), one finding reported twice: the Node check's `stop()` case waited for `ECONNREFUSED` inside the 1 s per-case bound, and Windows retries a loopback SYN that an RST refused for about 1 to 2 s before it reports the refusal, so the case would have failed on the Windows unit lane with a correct bridge. That case alone is now bounded by 5 s (the reason is in a comment and in D13.8); it still requires exactly `ECONNREFUSED`, and `stop()` must still resolve in under 900 ms. Evidence under Node 22.22.0: a scratch copy of the check whose refusal was reported 1.5 s late failed that case with the old 1 s bound ("did not finish within 1000 ms") and passed with the new one; a bundle whose `stop()` left the long poll unanswered still failed it, now on the 900 ms assertion ("stop took 1003 ms"). (3) The Windows reset that could erase a refusal's answer applies to the 401 check as well as the 413 one; the pending CI item above now names both and the response if CI shows it. Locally under Linux, 40 runs of each against the bundle sent no reset (Tcp `OutRsts` in `/proc/net/snmp` unchanged, against +10 for a control that closed ten connections with unread data) and every client read its answer; Windows remains unproven until the dispatched run. (4) Capabilities that `Computer.Capabilities` refuses were answered 409 unavailable without any log; they now log a fixed line without their values ("Computer bridge capabilities do not match their schema"), as a mismatched command result does, and the capabilities test expects that line for both wrong-shape answers (removing the line fails that test). Rerun after the fixes, Bun workloads through the lock wrapper from `packages/desktop` with `TMPDIR` and fresh XDG roots and VECTOR_TEST_HOME under `<scratch>/vector-D13/review`: `src/main/computer-bridge.test.ts` 33 passed, 0 failed (256 expects); `bun build ./src/main/computer-bridge.ts --target=node --format=esm` then `node ./test/computer/bridge-node.ts`: 7 of 7 checks passed; `bun typecheck` exits 0; Prettier clean on the three touched source and test files.
- D13 review fixes, second round (in the same commit; recorded in D13.1, D13.2, D13.6, D13.8, D13.12 and D13.13): (1) The `0ebc4203` CI item said no completed run there had failed and that full CI 41180504513 was in progress; it had completed with both unit jobs failing, and the item and the Next work line now say what that run shows, with the Linux `session-traffic.test.ts:486` failure triaged above. (2) The bridge logged through `console.warn`, which desktop main never writes to electron-log's files or the debug-log export (`initLogging` spies only the renderer console; no main-process console hook exists), so the capabilities line added in the first round would not have reached a user's exported logs. `createBridgeHandler` and `startComputerBridge` now take a `warn(message, ...fields)` that defaults to the console, every line goes through it, and D22 passes the log file's warn (D13.13); a new test gives a real bridge a `warn` and expects both a capabilities line and a command line there and none on the console (not passing `warn` to the handler fails it). (3) Nothing checked the bound address: the URL is built from the host constant. A new test expects `ECONNREFUSED` from `::1` and `127.0.0.2` on the bridge's port, each where the host can listen on it (this sandbox has no IPv6, so `127.0.0.2` alone here); `server.listen(0, ...)` without the host fails it. (4) A pipelined request's response never gets a `close` from Node, so a client that pipelined an act and an `acquire {wait: true}` and left cancelled only the act and left the late lease held (reproduced under Node 22.22.0 with a scratch probe against the bundle: cancels `[act]`, no release). The bridge now also listens for `request.socket`'s `close`, removed on every response close, so a keep-alive connection gathers no listeners; the probe then cancelled both and released lease 7. Regression tests: a handler-seam case whose connection closes under a queued acquire (cancel once, lease released, no listener left) and one whose connection closes after the answer (no cancel, no listener left); Node checks for the pipelined act and acquire (fails on the old bundle, "did not finish within 1000 ms") and for twelve sequential requests on one keep-alive connection (a bundle that kept its connection listeners fails it with `MaxListenersExceededWarning`). (5) A non-`ComputerError` rejection or a mismatched result after its client left or `stop()` answered was dropped unlogged; both are now logged before that early return, the handler test expects `["Computer bridge command failed", "act", "Error"]`, and a new case expects the mismatch line for an acquire answered `{lease: 0}` after its client left, with nothing released; D13.12 asks D19 to end a cancelled run with a `ComputerError` so a normal cancel logs nothing. Mutations, each restored by copying the file back from `<scratch>/vector-D13/mutations2` (checked with `cmp`), each failing only its own new or updated test: the wildcard bind, no connection `close` listener (the queued-connection case, by its 30 s timeout), the connection listener kept after the answer, late failures unlogged, late mismatches unlogged, and `warn` not passed to the handler. Rerun after the fixes, Bun workloads through the lock wrapper from `packages/desktop` with `TMPDIR` and fresh XDG roots and VECTOR_TEST_HOME under `<scratch>/vector-D13/review2`: `src/main/computer-bridge.test.ts` 38 passed, 0 failed (273 expects); `bun build ./src/main/computer-bridge.ts --target=node --format=esm` then `node ./test/computer/bridge-node.ts` under Node 22.22.0: 9 of 9 checks passed; `bun typecheck` (`tsgo -b`) exits 0; Prettier clean on the three touched source and test files and on this file; in `hands-deviations.md` its only differences are five older lines outside the D13 section, untouched here.

## Outstanding validation

The two Windows failures below did not recur in fresh full CI 40999656844 (see the D5b CI item); they are kept for history. Full run [40813327379](https://github.com/scr1pter/claudeondfsfdaf/actions/runs/40813327379), at `a227c869`, completed with two failing Windows jobs. All Linux jobs, both browser suites, the Node bundle, and Windows engine shards 1–5 passed.

1. Windows unit job `123248533002`: the unchanged `core/test/util/windows-powershell.test.ts` native-command fixture exceeded its deadline after successfully loading certificate/archive commands and hashing a file. Its child imports only Node utilities and the direct Windows PowerShell helper, not the database or Hands code. Preserve the deadline/assertions; a single failed-job retry is appropriate to distinguish transient runner timing. This is not yet confirmed as a flake.
2. Windows engine shard 6, job `123248533096`: `engine/test/local-mode/local-run.test.ts`, “refuses a V2 runner turn on a model off in Vector Local before a route is built, and picks none”, unexpectedly resolved the free `vector/north` model. The fixture now uses persisted `withLocal` policy for its refusal/selection phase, preserving its initial Local-off assertion and zero-outbound guard. The focused test passes all six assertions and engine typecheck passes. Fresh Windows/full CI is still required; the failing run predates this fix.

D5b integration details discovered during D5a review (all addressed in D5b; see deviations D5b.5 to D5b.10):

- Resolve mention option description/disabled localization keys before rendering.
- Normal queued sends preserve markers. Custom command sends use a file-only protocol; do not silently drop computer pills. Refuse that unsupported combination without clearing the draft unless command transport is deliberately extended with its generators/tests.
- Undo/fork currently ignores synthetic markers. Restore only valid existing markers using exact canonical mention text; do not infer app hints from arbitrary plain text or extend the fixed marker shape casually.
- Dictation currently flattens non-image pills; preserve computer pills when appending transcription.
- Open onboarding through `showComputerOnboarding` so immediate close-start disposal cancels pending work. First-step consent never enables Hands; Finish performs a new permission probe after older polls and rereads policy before enabling. Examples insert without sending and preserve existing drafts.

## Local validation limits

Run one Bun process at a time, only individual test files from their package directory and per-package `bun typecheck`. Browser fixture checking uses `bun typecheck:e2e` in `packages/app`. Inspect tests for nested Bun subprocesses before running them; CLI-help, migration-generator and Windows subprocess fixtures stay in CI when they would violate this limit. Never run local full suites, Turbo, Playwright or Electron, and never operate the real screen/input or OS privacy settings in tests.

Engine runtime uses Node in the desktop utility process. Keep Bun APIs confined to existing Bun-specific adapters and test/build code. Public schema changes require the prescribed generators, one at a time. Commit as `Vector <bot@vectordev.ai>`. Never read credential files or publish personal contact details.

### D22 implementation checkpoint

`computer-startup.ts` composes D13 to D21 after app readiness and before the sidecar spawns: availability (Darwin 23+, Windows build 19041+, unelevated, company policy on), the settings store and policy, the helper supervisor and native backend, grants, overlays, the session controller, frames, executor, take-over, D21 permissions, notifications and the bridge, whose URL and token reach the engine only in its start message (`spawnLocalServer` `startEnvironment`). `attach()` connects the engine's event stream, abort route, session ancestry and the banner's reply routes after the health check; leases are refused until that stream delivers its first event. ### D23 implementation checkpoint

`computer-retention.ts` deletes screenshots on the user's schedule, clamped by company policy (INV-C8). It sweeps 30 s after the engine's health check and then hourly. `task` also prunes the holder's conversation, and the child session that took the lease, at task end, only up to that moment. `session` schedules nothing. Delete all sends `{all: true}` to E13's prune route, which removes and vacuums, and returns its count. Deletions run one at a time; the prune request names no directory (D23.2). The reduced service keeps the schedule and Delete all where Hands cannot run. After each deletion that removed something, and every Delete all, trusted renderer windows receive the selection on `computer-screenshots-removed`. The app tombstones matching cached parts exactly as E13 did, fences pages fetched before the deletion, and reads touched conversations again after any read under way. E13's markers are never dropped. The desktop renderer holds no current-session (native) history cache; D23.5 records why that history needs nothing here. Deviations D23.1 to D23.6.

Carried obligations satisfied here: `deleteScreenshots` and retention (D22.6, D22.14); history-cache refresh after deletion with tombstones kept (E13.6, E13.8, D6b open risk, handoff section 4). No engine, SDK or generated client input changed, so no generator ran.

Focused serial checks, each file run alone from its package after the last source edit: desktop `computer-retention` 13 tests (53 assertions), `computer-startup` 23 (137), `computer-ipc` 5 (89), `computer-preload` 4 (12); app `screenshot-history` 7 (43), `server-session` 65 (125) and `brand-guard` 5 (25). Desktop (`tsgo -b`), app (`tsgo -b`) and browser-fixture (`typecheck:e2e`) typechecks pass. Temporary mutations, each restored: dropping the page fence failed only "a page read before the deletion finished cannot bring its screenshots back"; skipping the cached-copy change failed only the two tests that read the cached copy; refetching without waiting for a read under way failed only that ordering test; dropping the task-end cutoff failed only "task prunes at task end"; dropping the company clamp failed only "a company maximum clamps a longer choice"; notifying after a deletion that removed nothing failed only that notification test. The tests use injected fetchers, fake timers, fake engines and virtual helpers only: no OS permission, real input, capture, helper process or Electron window was used.

CI only, none run on D23 yet: the Linux and Windows desktop unit lanes (they pick up `computer-retention.test.ts` and the extended `computer-*` files), the app unit lanes (`server-session.test.ts`, `screenshot-history.test.ts`), the production benchmark after-run (`server-session.ts` gains the removal fence on its message-load path; the gate dispatches it against the latest baseline), and C8's real-Electron check that the deletion event reaches the main window through the context bridge. The D22 CI items listed above are still pending as well.

### M3 capture and geometry/source checkpoint

M3 adds the real asynchronous ScreenCaptureKit display/window/zoom adapter, permission-free CG display metadata, strict request/budget validation, local JPEG/PNG encoding, final-frame perceptual hashes and a bounded metadata-only capture registry. Screen preflight precedes every content enumeration/capture. A selected window uses its actual native filter scale and independent-window capture; minimized state is queried only for that approved target, separately from offscreen/occluded state. Display capture performs no AX minimization scan.

An explicit included-window source omits overlays, unapproved/protected apps, notifications and Vector's own raw UI. Native front-to-back geometry determines the visible opaque masks applied before encoding/hash. Vector contributes a static labelled block; hiddenCount uses distinct observed regular application owners, excluding notification/furniture hosts. Unresolved visible owners refuse unless a stable, valid Apple/platform non-application host witness proves only that an excluded opaque region contributes no app count. That witness never grants pixel/input access.

One uniform imageScale describes the actual cropped native source. Right/bottom slivers smaller than one output pixel are trimmed so integer output dimensions cannot introduce aspect-ratio padding or coordinate drift. Original window/process witnesses remain separate. Zoom rereads its original full-frame region at native scale within budget, emits PNG, retains the base frame and is never an input coordinate frame. Display/active-Space notifications invalidate geometry and send the existing display_change event. Every accepted authority update rotates a private observation epoch, so repeated wire session IDs/revisions cannot revive old captures.

Actual M3 compilation and native unit execution remain CI-only. The enforced measured 1.0.2 lock remains until a successful M3 build/reproducibility/core run supplies its candidate. ### M4 accessibility/source checkpoint

M4 adds all six existing UI operations: tree, hittest, focused, resolve, perform and setValue. A private AX queue walks at most 400 nodes/depth 30, bounds child-array queries before allocation, detects cycles and leaves a soft cutoff before the dispatcher deadline. Native handles remain app-scoped and same-PID. Strings are control/bidi stripped and UTF-16 capped; secure values are never fetched. Missing security proof refuses instead of treating a failed query as a nonsecure field.

The bounded registry binds opaque refs to worker/snapshot generation, private authority epoch, process start/path, actual AX equality, window/frame and visibility. Resolve preserves its exact original ref. Display/Space/authority/worker transitions retire metadata. Independent-window captures retain their observed target for AX hit tests even when offscreen/occluded; display frames use current native order. Actual AX containing-window observations disambiguate overlapping windows, and mutation repeats this check inside the current authority fence to reject same-PID reparenting.

Protected/unapproved observations stop at actual CG owner/window metadata and return a window-level structural descriptor without any AX hit, label, value, ref or document lookup. Approved mutations reobserve native ownership, phase and security immediately before posting. Readback occurs outside the dispatcher lock and requires complete text, so a truncated prefix cannot produce took=true. Only complete local document URLs may become known canonical paths; absent or unsupported evidence stays unknown. Temporary manual accessibility wake is limited to an actually mapped, valid same-team Electron framework and restores its original false state through idempotent bounded cleanup. Unknown runtime proof leaves the setting untouched.

The 22 pure registry and 32 injected service groups await actual native compiler/unit execution. No OS input/capture/trust request or owner application was used locally. ### M5 input/source checkpoint

M5 adds the seven native input handlers, a private explicitly flagged/tagged CG event source, actual layout/IME/data/keyboard-type fingerprints, physical-code versus semantic-character resolution, and scalar-safe Unicode chunks of at most 20 UTF-16 units. Approved hit results now carry actual bounded layout/menu facts for the existing main/core shortcut gate. The helper repeats the key floor against actual semantics/effective modifiers; raw or resolved/custom-menu paste remains text for secure-field and developer refusal. Unrepresentable layout facts fail closed.

Each operation owns one bounded held-input lease. Downs reserve matching ups before posting; false/uncertain posts, cancellation, deadlines and late callbacks retain/release only their own state. Multi-modifier ups unwind prior explicit flags correctly, and pointer mouseups retain requested Shift/Command semantics. The journal performs bounded reverse cleanup without reentering AX/authority. It never retargets keyboard ups to a reused PID/current focus; owned global button ups retain their last point. Failed cleanup remains pending and cannot return a successful hold or start fresh input.

AX focus/security/ref/menu observations occur outside the short posting fence. Each commit rechecks current authority, permission, prepared-fact age and actual CG window/process/frame/alpha; every type chunk/key repeat/post retains its private process/AX baseline and layout fence. The independent hold timer is armed on the first down and releases before completion checks. Display/active-Space callbacks release input locally and cancel pending native geometry work before sending the desktop event. Exact trusted click-through overlay IDs are skipped; Vector main windows stay protected. No background feature is advertised before M9.

### M7 app control, hosted ownership and clipboard source checkpoint

Apps and program launch now use actual native running/static signatures, current session policy, bounded canonical artifact fingerprints and one-use private launch tokens. Protected paths are refused before native resolution, including canonical aliases and prepared-path replacements.

The shared resolver requires narrow verified benign host classification, stable responsibility, real scoped AX equality and current private process/authority witnesses. Capture, geometry, AX refs/actions, input and app catalog/focus consume those facts without relabeling process identities. Security/unknown hosts and unprovable remote proxies remain refused. The optional native clipboard stores a bounded complete pasteboard loan, preserves all materialized types and restores only owned counts on explicit original-text restoration or lifecycle cleanup. Provider timeouts quarantine the worker; stale generations and same-ID replacement cannot reuse old loans. Native writes are selected by desktop feature support while Main keeps its task-owned hash. Cleanup and new clipboard admission are ordered without waiting under Dispatcher authority. A bounded request-ingress queue keeps EOF visible during a stalled native commit; an independent exit fuse and supervisor grace preserve the process-lifecycle bound. Synchronous OS IPC and lack of pasteboard compare-and-swap remain documented platform limits.

### M7 compiler and catalog authority repair

Native run [41963345909](https://github.com/scr1pter/claudeondfsfdaf/actions/runs/41963345909) at `50f08508` fails compilation before native units/core/reproducibility. Actual diagnostics identify four Swift import/type issues: qualified Darwin.stat resolves the struct constructor, strict/all-architecture Security flags lack those inferred member names, strdup's environment-array inference needs explicit Swift strings/C-string conversion, and the host policy referenced the wrong generated identity enum. The repair uses lstat (the unused follow-links branch is removed), the SDK's real kSecCS flags with no-network validation retained, explicit CString storage and the existing generated Identity enum. No measured 1.0.6 candidate is accepted from this failed run.

Before, "Build both channel helper architectures" took 314-342 s on fast runners and 514-620 s on slow ones (both timeouts, 616 s and 620 s, slow-group on the new image), so each per-architecture build ran at about 255-345 s on the slow half. Only the Windows source digest changes, and because `VECTOR_COMPUTER_SOURCE_DIGEST` is a build define the Windows exe hash changes for that reason alone. After (to fill from Windows CI): at least 3 windows-build jobs including one slow-group job, logged processor count 4, step 9 at or below about 420 s (fast group at or below about 260 s). A slow-group step 9 above about 470 s means this is not enough and the next lever (a measured per-phase budget or precompiled headers, with a helperVersion bump and re-measured macOS lock) goes to the lead as its own package.

That stage means CreateForWindow returned success after its full 50 ms item ticket had expired while the 750 ms acquisition deadline was still current (`capture_native.cpp` window_bitmap, line 249).
