The 10 skips are apps-installed-target (target_not_installed), eight input_detection_unavailable rows (UIA counter and value, secure field, tagged click, Unicode typing, stale capture, owned input release, input detection probe) and hardware-user-input-pauses (hardware_input_required). [Capture provenance](./evidence/42138851647-windows-capture-provenance.json) binds both report ZIPs to their API digests and raw report hashes.

Staged step 19 of the same job now reaches Electron. Its 28 closed receipts are identical in the preserved log and the job log: bootstrap restricted/u/n/r, boot, ready, properties, a 4/4/4/4 native region proof, every controller step through native-proof, and the ASAR capability receipt. The driver result frame then reports stop native_failure with exit 1, 1.95 s after native-proof and far inside the parent's 20 s kill deadline. native-os-stop and native-capture-exclusion fail native_proof_invalid through the Electron smoke's catch after `require(proof.stop.ok)` (`computer-overlay-smoke-electron.ts:795`, catch at 805-807); the 15 generic checks pass with overlay-workspaces not_applicable, and the final cause is assertion_failed. The receipts do not record whether the failed result arrived before or after the driver's ready frame (the controller step native-ready is overlay readiness), and the native failure cause is undiagnosed. A read-only source observation, not a fix: `overlay_driver.cpp:275` discards the boolean returned by `ensure_owned_restricted`, which returns false for an existing grant, true after a verified repair and otherwise throws. Inference only: the boot receipts after restricted/u/n/r are therefore consistent with a verified repair, but no receipt records it. [Staged provenance](./evidence/42138851647-windows-staged-provenance.json) records all 24 job steps, the three-artifact catalog, the receipts and the stage manifest: helper 1.0.15, overlay stage digest `0267d46b` (not the measured helper identity) and 79 files.

Regular run [42138877213](https://github.com/scr1pter/claudeondfsfdaf/actions/runs/42138877213), windows-build job 127633948612, reports core 30 passed/0 failed/1 skipped and isolated 35 passed/0 failed/11 skipped in its step 11 and step 12 log totals. Observed: step 12 printed no throw or error after its totals. Derived from the step source (`computer-helper.yml:721` throws on any nonzero status), not printed in the log: its self-test command exited 0. The generic Electron smoke in step 15 passes 15 checks with 3 named skips. Staged step 18 prints the same 28 receipts as the fast run, then throws "Actual isolated overlay Stop failed or required native evidence is unavailable."; windows-arm64-test 127638361565 is skipped. Its reports artifact 13467212708 (1343 bytes, expires 2026-11-20) is API metadata only because its storage host is not reachable from this environment, so no isolated row is verified; step 25's CI upload lines witness the same size and digest. Inference only, not observed: 35/11/0 over 46 rows is consistent with capture-color-patch passing and the aggregate privileged row becoming a skip, which would suggest intermittent capture timing; it is not the only explanation. [Regular provenance](./evidence/42138877213-windows-provenance.json) keeps these log rows with their step attribution.

Artifacts-only preview [42140933292](https://github.com/scr1pter/claudeondfsfdaf/actions/runs/42140933292) ran on `hands` with tag v1.99.112, dry_run true, allow_unsigned true and prerelease false. Prepare job 127640777343 passed the HANDS_DRY_RUN_GUARD in step 3, skipped the protected-environment step 4 and ran the signing-policy step 9 with RELEASE_DRY_RUN true (its unsigned dry-run branch writes only to the step output and summary, so that branch is source-derived). Of 34 jobs, 26 succeeded, 1 failed and 7 were skipped: the catalog, credentials preflight, six typechecks, 9 of 10 test jobs and both build smokes passed. Test (desktop) job 127641245309 step 7 reports "Desktop: 181/183 test files passed". The one failing case in `computer-overlay-workflow.test.ts` (5 passed/1 failed), "native prerequisite absence never swallows an assertion failure or authorizes a high-token input fallback", fails `not /continue-on-error|exit 0|elevatedToken/` on `exit 0`. Helper jobs pass. mac-build 127641332772 built the universal helper twice with identical shipping bytes, enforced the measured lock and cleaned up its temporary CI certificate; mac-test macos-26 127646312950 and macos-15-intel 127646313054 report 31 passed/0 failed/1 skipped. windows-build 127641332829 found no earlier helper artifact, rebuilt both architectures from source and reports x64 core 30/0/1. windows-arm64-test 127646069154 ran on windows-11-arm (image windows-11-vs2026-arm64, OS 10.0.26200), checked the downloaded helper and test-executable digests, ran unit, `--version` with arch arm64 and both probes, and reports "Computer helper checks: 30 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed". The Windows reports are byte-identical across jobs, as are the Mac reports, and carry no architecture or run, so each report is tied to its job only by that job's log lines. overlay-linux and desktop-e2e-linux are skipped by design because full-desktop-e2e false sets the plan's desktop output false, which also skips the generic overlay and stage steps; isolated-desktop false skips every isolated scenario step and the actual Stop steps. The preview's root cause is source-level: `c5c1515b` added `exit 0` after the permitted 77 summary in computer-helper.yml's Windows Stop step and the staged `--native-stage` x64 diagnostic to test.yml, without updating these two contract tests (last changed in `d98ee867` and `40f812d0`). Both workflow changes are intended per [hands-deviations.md](./hands-deviations.md) "C8 follow-up" item 3. Bun stopped the x64 case at its first failing expect, so its later three-upload expectation, against the job's four upload steps, was never evaluated and also needs updating.

Not proven: native Stop, a stable capture pass, isolated or packaged ARM64 behavior, immutable restoration across attempts, final package hashes and signatures, compiled Windows Main pins, Linux helper absence, updater metadata and byte audits. macOS and Windows packaging have never executed on this lane; Linux packaging executed only once, successfully, in preview [42053349008](https://github.com/scr1pter/claudeondfsfdaf/actions/runs/42053349008) (job 127341263064) at `b86d5dbd`, an earlier source. The fast-run artifacts expire 2026-11-13, the regular and preview artifacts 2026-11-20 and the preview's model catalog 2026-12-06. Deferred to later packages: the two contract tests, a new preview, the native Stop diagnosis and fix, and capture measurement and fix. No release credit.

## H3 artifacts-only preview 42454176369 through packaging

H3 (2026-11-08) spent its single preview dispatch, allowed by the owner's approval of 04:15 UTC ([hands-deviations.md](./hands-deviations.md) "H2" item 4), on preview [42454176369](https://github.com/scr1pter/claudeondfsfdaf/actions/runs/42454176369) at `420912fb` (run 102, attempt 1). Guard and gates, observed. The macOS and Windows jobs printed their unsigned-skip lines, and every Azure and publisher input of the Windows sign step was empty. Publish 128656734853 was skipped without steps. Of 34 jobs, 29 succeeded, 1 failed and 4 were skipped (overlay-linux and desktop-e2e-linux by design, the final ARM64 verifier and Publish). The helper jobs passed: plan; mac-build 128650411445 (no earlier artifact, built twice, core 31/0/1, lock enforced, temporary certificate cleaned up); windows-build 128650411398 (no earlier artifact, core 30/0/1); windows-arm64-test 128652465162 on windows-11-arm (image windows-11-vs2026-arm64, OS 10.0.26200; core 30/0/1); and both mac-test jobs.

Per platform:
- Linux desktop apps 128651522063: success, the first Linux packaging at this source. One package attempt built x64 and arm64 AppImages. verify-notices printed "Artifact audit: 191 files ... violations: 0" for each unpacked app. Step 8 runs under `set -euo pipefail`, starts with `test ! -e` on both `resources/computer` folders, and checks both latest-linux yml files and the AppImage architectures; its success shows the helper is absent (derived from the step source and conclusion). Artifact 13607526860 (360,843,803 bytes).
- macOS desktop apps 128653871810 on macos-15-arm64: success, the first macOS packaging on this lane. The darwin-source restore printed "Restored the exact helper artifact ids, source/run identity and safe archives."; the prebuilt source, cdhash and lock check (step 7) succeeded silently; one package attempt each for arm64 and x64; "finalized latest yml files"; four audits with 0 violations and four "has one consistent compiled, bundle, update channel and computer helper" lines; and two "Verified the final computer helper identity, signatures, provenance and packaged self-test." lines. Step 16 (ad-hoc app signatures, helper codesign, DMGs present, both zips in latest-mac.yml) succeeded. Artifact 13608360351 (737,158,415 bytes).
- Windows desktop apps 128653871818 on windows-2025: failure at step 15, the first Windows packaging on this lane. Steps 6-14 passed: the win32-source restore, "No earlier helper artifact exists in this run." for the final bytes, the unsigned skip, the sign/verify step (pin map x64 `b295af0f...`, arm64 `92dcbb3a...`), the final-bytes artifact 13607507003, the build, one combined NSIS package attempt, two audits with 0 violations, three NotSigned installers and latest.yml (derived from step 14's success). Step 15's helper-signature loop passed; then the x64 final verifier threw "The packaged Main JavaScript files are missing or ambiguous." from `extractWindowsHelperCompiledPins`. Steps 16-19 were skipped, so no ARM64 proof or Windows platform artifact exists.
- Final ARM64 verifier 128656660944: not observed (skipped because Windows desktop apps failed).

Root cause, reproduced locally: @electron/asar 3.4.1, the pinned reader, names entries with the host path separator. `listPackage` builds names with `path.join("/", name)`, which on Windows gives `\out\main\index.js`, and a nested `statFile`/`extractFile` splits its argument on `path.sep`. The extractor filtered the listing with a `/`-only pattern. Its only test runs on Linux Test (desktop), and the file is not on the Windows unit lane. The final ARM64 verifier calls the same function. Inference only: it would have failed the same way.

Fix: committed separately as `fix(hands): read packed Windows Main pins with the asar separator`. When this section was written (2026-11-08), the fix was in the working tree only. This preview ran `420912fb`, before the fix, so it does not CI-confirm it; that confirmation is owed to H3a (H8's preview unless the owner approves an extra H3 dispatch). `extractWindowsHelperCompiledPins` (`packages/desktop/scripts/verify-computer-helper-windows.ts`) now maps listed names from the reader's separator to `/` before the unchanged checks, rejects a duplicated name as ambiguous, and reads each entry with the separator restored. An optional `{asar, separator}` dependency, the file's existing injection pattern, lets the new case drive the real reader over a real archive with Windows naming rules. The new case is "the release x64 verifier reads the packed Main pins through the asar reader's Windows backslash names". It first asserts that the resolved reader is @electron/asar 3.4.1, so its simulated naming rules stay tied to the pinned reader, and its wrapper hands the real reader `path.sep` names, so on a Windows host it reads the real archive unchanged. It makes verify-computer-helper-windows.test.ts 17/0 with 81 expect calls (16/0 with 78 at `420912fb`). Three mutations were each applied by edit and reverted by its exact inverse, and the diff was confirmed after each revert; all three were re-run after the version assertion and the `path.sep` wrapper were added, with the same results:
- removing the listing normalization fails the case with "missing or ambiguous" (16/1);
- reading with the `/` name fails it with "was not found in this archive" (16/1);
- removing the duplicate check fails its rejection (16/1).

verify-computer-helper.test.ts 17/0 and computer-release-workflow.test.ts 8/0 still pass, and the desktop typecheck passes. The file is not a COMPUTER_HELPER_BUILD_INPUTS file and is not in any source digest, so the measured macOS lock is untouched.

Artifacts: 13 in total, all API metadata only with CI upload witnesses. The session proxy refused this run's artifact storage host (403 connect_rejected) for four attempted downloads; network access is owner-gated and was not requested, so the read-only compiled-pin cross-check was not possible. The installer artifacts are 13608360351 (macOS) and 13607526860 (Linux), and 13607507003 holds the final Windows helper bytes (1,981,181 bytes). Digests are in the provenance record.

Watch point: on windows-11-arm, `bun install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts` took 227.99 s in windows-arm64-test. The never-run final ARM64 verifier has a 15-minute job timeout and installs through the composite setup-bun.

Split out:
- **H3a — Confirm the Windows final verifiers and the ARM64 proof.** Goal: the Windows x64 final verifier, steps 16-19 (ARM64 proof restore check, archive, proof and platform uploads) and the final ARM64 verifier job run green after the separator fix is committed and pushed. Files: none beyond the fix unless a new failure appears there. Done when: a preview at the pushed head shows Windows desktop apps and "Verify the final ARM64 helper and actual packed Main pins" succeeding, with Publish still skipped. CI confirmation is owed to H8's preview unless the owner approves an extra H3 dispatch.
- **H3b — Run the Windows verifier tests on the Windows x64 unit lane.** Goal: verify-computer-helper-windows.test.ts runs on a Windows x64 unit lane (for example the computer-helper-windows-units job), so `extractWindowsHelperCompiledPins` with the default pinned reader and `path.sep` is exercised on real Windows before the next preview; today the file runs only on Linux Test (desktop), and `grep` finds no reference to it in test.yml. Files: .github/workflows/test.yml, coordinated with H2 and H6 because that file is conflict-heavy (7 of the predicted conflict hunks with main). H3 does not change test.yml. The file's other cases have never run on Windows, so they may need host-aware fixtures; that is not assessed here. Done when: a fast x64 diagnostic or regular Windows run at the pushed head reports the file passing on Windows.

Not proven: compiled Windows Main pins, the final x64 and ARM64 packaged helper checks, the packaged ARM64 self-test, immutable restoration across attempts (no attempt 2), signed or notarized builds (Developer ID, Authenticode), launching the installers on clean machines, update-feed behaviour, native Stop and capture, isolated ARM64 scenarios and owner OS checks. The preview reuses version 1.99.112, which main already published; the version decision is owner-gated. No release credit.


## H4 closed Windows staged Stop driver stages

H4 (2026-11-08) adds a closed, failure-only location to the never-shipped Windows overlay driver, so the staged native Stop's native_failure can name the step that threw. It does not fix the failure (that is H5) and changes no authority, deadline, outcome, exit code or version. When this entry was written (2026-11-08 08:42 UTC), H4 was in the working tree only, on top of `63193992` (equal to `origin/hands`): by the orchestrator's instruction it was not committed, pushed or run in CI. Results after the push are appended below this section's text, not edited into it. The design is [hands-deviations.md](./hands-deviations.md) "C8 follow-up" item 4, and the decisions are deviation "H4" items 1-10. A review of this working-tree state was then fixed in place (2026-11-08 09:10 UTC, still uncommitted); the bullets below say which facts are from the first draft and which are after the review fixes.

The change:
- `overlay_driver.h` gains the stage enum and its 29 names, the five HRESULT buckets, optional `stage` and `cause` fields on `OverlayDriverResult`, the exported `DriverFailure`/`DriverSkip`, and the pure classifiers `overlay_driver_stage_name`, `overlay_driver_stage`, `overlay_driver_cause`, `overlay_driver_hresult_class` and `overlay_driver_caught`.
- `overlay_driver.cpp`: `run_overlay_driver` stores dpi, parent and files, and `click_stop` stores each later stage before its fallible step, as plain enum assignments; the post's single condition is unchanged, with observe-down stored only once the post was accepted. The single `catch (...)` (line 449 after the review fixes) classifies the exception with the old ok/skipped/reason mapping, a core ProtocolError included, and falls back to the old key-less native_failure if classification itself throws. `wire()` adds both keys only for a coherent unskipped failure with closed values, and a non-driver cause only with native_failure.
- `computer-overlay-smoke-report.ts` gains the closed mirrors (stages, causes, native codes, HRESULT buckets and the 18 driver reasons, which now open `COMPUTER_SMOKE_NATIVE_REASONS`), `computerSmokeDriverFailure` and the pure frame check `parseComputerSmokeDriverResultFrame(value, platform)`. The result receipt's `stop` may carry the two keys, only when the receipt is read on win32; `capture` and the top level may not. `computer-overlay-smoke-native.ts` calls the frame check, so it accepts the keys only in a win32 result frame (any mismatch is stream_invalid, a failure), and forwards them in the receipt. Darwin frames and receipts reject them, and the proof value, report rows and exit expectation never read them. `computer-overlay-smoke.ts:371`, the parent's receipt re-parse, now passes `process.platform`.
- No COMPUTER_HELPER_BUILD_INPUTS file, identity.json, blocklist.json, macOS source, icon, packages/desktop/package.json, publishedDesktopVersion or release-199112.md changed (`git diff --name-only`), so the measured macOS 1.0.15 lock is untouched. The Windows source digest changes, as for any Windows source change; Windows has no lock.

Source-derived narrowing (derived from `63193992`, not observed): the stop-mode writer is created after the DPI and parent steps, so a throw there leaves no result frame; the three runs' well-formed frames therefore exclude SetThreadDpiAwarenessContext. Every driver reason thrown in stop mode is coherent, so wire()'s sanitization was unreachable. The native_failure came from a non-driver exception (a core Failure, a winrt::hresult_error, a std exception or another type) at stage files or later. Inference only: candidates include fixture_files, observing the elevated bootstrap from the restricted medium-token driver (windows_identity throws std::runtime_error), guardian startup, UIA creation and the banner window observation; none is presumed. Inference only: files already observes the driver's own process, so a later observe-parent failure would point at the Electron parent (or a change in the driver's re-observed identity), not at the first token read of the driver itself.

## HF-1 — native-stop-fix merged at helper 1.0.15 (15 November 2026)

Lane `hands-finish` starts from `release-finish` `0ee486e3412c29512022d369325a3747b567d090`, the PR #33 head when the lane opened. The [deviation entry](./hands-deviations.md#hf-1--windows-only-diagnostics-keep-helper-1015) records why helper `1.0.15` is kept.

**Merge.** The HF-1 merge commit `6179bd42835ae279d019f8c4669ca576e3489678` (tree `0ea5f29310f3cf74ad6736483d54880f93ddaed1`) has first parent `0ee486e3` and second parent `a0fbfa3b16b65c0def4459d83892ee1f09c71daa`, the `origin/native-stop-fix` tip (merge-base `f830e3fd`). It is a `--no-ff` merge, with no rebase or cherry-pick, of six commits: `8e792a72`, `503418a1`, `d3d39430`, `78f1a459`, merge `8d99c2bb` and `a0fbfa3b`. Before merging, `git merge-tree --write-tree` exited 0 in every case checked:

- against `0ee486e3`, tree `0ea5f29310f3cf74ad6736483d54880f93ddaed1`;
- against the then-current `origin/release-finish` `cf2373c4`, which already contains the CI-fix lane's pushed commits, tree `69ea41345d1b37cc617c2f34be035d1aaeae21cb`;
- against `origin/main` `58f85edc` (PR #33 not merged), tree `c94a4ffb3355cf5975071c7f4d9af85826dd89d7`;
- against the local review-fixes ref `dcd781d0`, which has no remote branch, tree `9fe89e7fe15e728f91f4577d4b40a7288be18d3f`.

None of the 13 commits from `0ee486e3` to `cf2373c4` touches `test.yml` or `computer-workflow.test.ts`. The merge tree equals the predicted `0ea5f293…`. It brings 16 files (+412/−31): the `test.yml` macos-measure mode, both Hands ledgers, the new [diagnostic note](./hands-uia-timeout-diagnostic.md), `identity.json`, seven Windows native sources and tests, `computer-helper-selftest-windows.ts` (`WINDOWS_NATIVE_STAGES` grows from 199 to 219 stages) and three desktop script tests. **Restore.** The follow-up commit restores `packages/desktop/native/computer-helper/identity.json` byte for byte from `0ee486e3`, at helper `1.0.15`. `macos/identity.lock.json` is untouched. Invariant 2, the `git diff --stat 0ee486e3` over `macos/`, `identity.json`, `blocklist.json`, the channel `icon.icns` files and the nine `COMPUTER_HELPER_BUILD_INPUTS`, listed only `identity.json` for the merge tree and prints nothing for the restored tree. `0ee486e3`'s mac-build job `132574657008` (run `43636452606`) passed both `Enforce the measured production identity lock` and `Require the measured macOS helper version and toolchain for this channel`. Every recorded count, run, job, artifact and hash is kept as written.

**Diagnostic scope.** The merged diagnostics are:

- (a) the first UIA phase seen at a timeout is kept immutable, for `stop-root-retired` / `core_failure:no_ui_tree` (`8e792a72`, with the sentinel fix in `503418a1`);

## HF-2 — MCP and PTY isolation-order tests (15 November 2026)

HF-2 starts at `acd0c9bb` on `hands-finish`, after the HF-1 merge `6179bd42` and its restore. It adds the PTY and MCP halves of spawn-review tests S6-5 and S6-7 and answers the §4.2 "Not checked" item in [v2-spawn-review.md](./v2-spawn-review.md). It is test-only: no file under `packages/*/src` changed, including `engine/src/mcp/index.ts`, `engine/src/mcp/stdio.ts`, `core/src/pty.ts` and `core/src/process-isolation.ts`. C5 (`core/pty.ts:240-242`), C6 (`mcp/index.ts:485-488`), C7 and C8 (`renderer-status.ts:15`) were already resolved and are unchanged. The [deviation entry](./hands-deviations.md#hf-2--isolation-order-tests-ask-the-launch-floor-and-use-its-loader-guard) records the corrected premises and the choices below.

**§4.2 answer.** `localTransport` (`engine/mcp/stdio.ts:13`) runs `ProcessIsolation.launch` inside `Effect.promise` (`mcp/index.ts:489`), so a launch-floor refusal is a defect inside `connectLocal`. `MCP.create`'s `Effect.catchCause` (`:553-559`) re-interrupts a cause that holds only interrupts and turns every other cause into `{ status: "failed", error: message }`. The new MCP regression case proves it, so no MCP code changed.

**New files.**

- `packages/engine/test/fixture/mcp-required-isolation.ts` is a child fixture that calls `ProcessIsolation.requireIsolation()` at load. It builds the real MCP layer as `test/mcp/isolation.test.ts` does: `TestConfig`, a mocked `McpAuth`, a trusted workspace, the real spawner and a Node stdio server that writes a marker before its handshake. It uses no fake transport and no module mock. Its four cases are:
  - **S6-7, inherited and loader environments.** Under `computer: { enabled: false }`, `python3 <instance>/computer_control_mcp.py <marker>` ends as `{ status: "failed", error: <the policy's text> }`. That text contains neither `ProcessIsolation.REFUSAL`, `/usr/bin/bwrap`, `--die-with-parent` nor the floor's own message, there is no client, and the marker never appears. The loader variant first asserts that the launch floor refuses the same argv and environment.
  - **An allowed command follows ProcessIsolation's own launch decision.** Where `launch` refuses the Node server (off Linux), the status is failed with exactly that message, there is no client or marker, and the fixture prints `MCP_REQUIRED_ISOLATION_LAUNCH=refused`. Where it plans plainly (Linux while `FLOOR` is false), nothing starts and the fixture prints `=planned`.
  - **The §4.2 regression.** With a loader environment, startup and a second `connect` both end as failed with the floor's message, with no client, no tools and no marker, and no defect escapes.
- `packages/engine/test/mcp/computer-policy-isolation.test.ts` is the parent. It runs `bun test --timeout 30000 ./test/fixture/mcp-required-isolation.ts` from `packages/engine`, kills the child at 50 s, allows 60 s, and expects exit 0, `4 pass`, ` 0 fail` and a `MCP_REQUIRED_ISOLATION_LAUNCH=planned|refused` line. It has no platform gate.
- `packages/core/test/pty/pty-required-isolation.test.ts` respawns itself with `VECTOR_TEST_PTY_REQUIRED_ISOLATION=1` and reuses `pty-session.test.ts`'s layer (`Pty` and `EventV2` with a mocked `Config` and a fixed `Location`). Its child cases, both `it.live`, start a terminal whose environment sets `LD_PRELOAD=refused-test-loader`:
  - **Control.** A passing `beforeSpawn` is followed by the floor's own refusal. The order is `["before-spawn"]`, `pty.list()` is empty and there is no marker.
  - **S6-5.** A `beforeSpawn` that dies reports its own message, which is neither REFUSAL nor the floor's message. The order is `["before-spawn"]`, `pty.list()` is empty and there is no marker.
  - The parent case is skipped on Windows, as every PTY suite is, and in its own child process. It is a gated top-level `test.skipIf`, not an `else` branch.

**Which branch each host takes.**

- **This sandbox and test.yml Linux.** Only the loader variant of S6-7 decides the order. The allowed command takes the `planned` branch, the §4.2 refusal is the loader guard, and the PTY test runs in the Linux unit lane.
- **test.yml Windows engine shards.** Both S6-7 variants decide the order. The allowed command takes the `refused` branch with REFUSAL, the §4.2 refusal is still the loader guard, and the PTY test is skipped.
- test.yml has no macOS unit or engine lane.

**Mutation checks.** Each was a temporary edit, reverted at once with `git checkout`; `git diff --stat HEAD -- 'packages/*/src'` printed nothing afterwards.

- Running `ProcessIsolation.launch` before `beforeSpawn` in `pty.ts` failed both PTY child cases. S6-5 received the loader message, and the control case recorded no recheck.
- Moving the MCP policy check after `localTransport` failed only the loader S6-7 variant (3 passed, 1 failed).
- Making `MCP.create`'s `catchCause` re-raise every cause as a defect failed only the §4.2 case (3 passed, 1 failed).

**Local checks.** Bun 1.3.14 ran under the serial lock, one file per command, each with `bun test --timeout 30000`, with `TMPDIR`, `XDG_*` and `VECTOR_TEST_HOME` under `<scratch>/hf2/`. In this record, `<scratch>` stands for a local temporary scratch folder (the HF entries' folder was not kept), and `<worktree>` is the lane's worktree.

- From `packages/engine`:
  - `test/mcp/computer-policy.test.ts`: 25 passed / 0 failed, 161 assertions;
  - `test/mcp/isolation.test.ts`: 4 / 0, 21 assertions;
  - `test/mcp/lifecycle.test.ts`: 42 / 0, 139 assertions;
  - the new `test/mcp/computer-policy-isolation.test.ts`: 1 / 0, 4 assertions, with the child printing `planned`;
  - the fixture run directly as `./test/fixture/mcp-required-isolation.ts`: 4 / 0, 19 assertions. The bare form `test/fixture/mcp-required-isolation.ts` exits 1 with "did not match any test files", because Bun reads it as a name filter.
- From `packages/core`:
  - `test/pty/pty-session.test.ts`: 9 / 0, 36 assertions;
  - the new `test/pty/pty-required-isolation.test.ts`: 1 / 0, 3 assertions. Its child mode, run directly with `VECTOR_TEST_PTY_REQUIRED_ISOLATION=1`, gave 2 passed / 1 skipped (the parent case) / 0 failed, 10 assertions.

The first engine `bun typecheck` failed on the fixture: `toEqual` rejected a `status` widened to `string`, which `bun test` does not check. The fix was `"failed" as const`, after which engine `bun typecheck` exited 0. Core `bun typecheck` exited 0, and the full tree `bun turbo typecheck --concurrency=2` passed 27 of 27 tasks. Prettier `--check` passes on the three new files. `git diff --check` passes, and `git diff --stat HEAD -- 'packages/*/src'` prints nothing. The three-way merge of `v2-spawn-review.md` with `origin/review-fixes` (`1433d105`) and with `origin/release-finish` (`cf2373c4`), both from merge-base `0ee486e3`, is clean. Neither branch touches this record or the deviations log after that base. On this record and the deviations log, Prettier reports the same findings as at `acd0c9bb` (123 and 20 changed lines), and the HF-2 edits add none. On `v2-spawn-review.md` it reports 86 changed lines, the same as at `acd0c9bb`. All of them are in tables that were already unaligned at `acd0c9bb`, including the S6 table whose S6-5 and S6-7 rows HF-2 extends.

**Deferred.**

- **Windows results.** These cover the MCP parent's `refused` branch, the inherited-environment S6-7 variant deciding the order, and the first run of the `./`-relative fixture path on Windows. test.yml runs on push only for `main` and `v2`, so a push to `hands-finish` starts nothing. After the HF-2 commit is pushed, the route is a `test.yml` dispatch on ref `hands-finish` with default inputs; with no exclusive input set, `unit` (`test.yml:548`), `engine-linux` (`:745`) and `engine-windows` (`:797`) all run. If the lane PR opens first, its `pull_request` run supplies the same lanes. The Windows result is pending, with no run id yet. On Linux the allowed-command case takes the `planned` branch and makes no assertion of its own, so Bun counts it as a pass with zero assertions and the parent accepts either line. HF-2 is not described as fully validated until HF-4, or the lane PR's `test.yml` run, records a Windows engine-shard log showing `MCP_REQUIRED_ISOLATION_LAUNCH=refused`. Until review round two of HF-5 (16 November) no passing run could show that line: the parent captured the child's stdout and printed it only on a failure. The parent now prints `MCP_REQUIRED_ISOLATION_LAUNCH=<branch> childElapsedMs=<n>` after its assertions pass (locally `planned childElapsedMs=3215`, the whole file 5.50 s). The first Windows engine-shard run records that line and the child's elapsed time here. If `childElapsedMs` is above 40,000 (80% of the 50 s kill), the cases are folded into the `.test.ts` file behind an environment gate, as `shell-required-isolation.test.ts` does (deviation point 5), instead of raising the kill or the test timeout.
- **Platform gaps.** There is no macOS lane, and the Windows PTY half is not run because of the PTY suites' gate.
- **Other lanes.** The shell halves of S6-5 (background and foreground shells) stay with the spawn test lanes; on Windows `ShellJobs.isolationRefusal` refuses before the recheck. The optional second recheck (C1), SPAWN-1/2/3 and S4-3/S4-5 also stay with their lanes.
- **This record.** The top block of this checkpoint is unchanged; it stays with the lane's follow-up docs package.

## HF-5 — Windows apps-running-target: detection-pause ordering (16 November 2026)

HF-5 root-causes the Windows `privileged.apps-running-target` failure from its printed stage and the code, and changes the isolated self-test so the failure cannot recur from that cause. Only the isolated self-test's row ordering changed: one settle step now runs between row 0 and row 1 of `--self-test privileged` with `VECTOR_COMPUTER_TEST_ISOLATED=1`. The Dispatcher, `WindowService` and `UserInputService` are unchanged, and so is the product's fail-closed pause behaviour; the commit title's "running-app inventory" is the self-test row `apps-running-target`, not the product's `apps.list`. It started at `272ccb6d0ee33f07d6c14394e41b7eddd73ad9e5`, which equalled `origin/hands-finish`. Under the step brief its changes were first left uncommitted in the lane worktree, and a review round then made the changes listed in point 10 of the deviation entry. The commit gate then committed them as `fix(hands): keep the Windows running-app inventory from failing on a Dispatcher pause cancellation` for `hands-finish`. No HF-5 CI run had finished and no new evidence file existed when this entry was written. Line numbers below are at that commit. [Deviation entry](./hands-deviations.md#hf-5--the-isolated-self-test-settles-its-unavailable-detection-verdict-before-session-bound-rows).

**Gate.** HF-4 printed the stage of every failure: `apps-running-target` failed at `apps-list-reply` / `paused` on x64 in runs [43809140577](https://github.com/scr1pter/claudeondfsfdaf/actions/runs/43809140577) and [43816713717](https://github.com/scr1pter/claudeondfsfdaf/actions/runs/43816713717), and on ARM64 in run 43809140577 (job `133112685091`). That is an `apps-list-*` stage with a nativeCode, so HF-5 proceeds.

**Where a `paused` reply can come from.** `apps-list-reply` is the fallback stage of `NativeScenario::success` (`windows/src/privileged_self_test.cpp:457-463`), reached from `running_target` (`:592-593`). It means `apps.list` replied `ok:false` with code `paused` and with a message that the origin mapping did not know. `apps.list` is registered non-mutating and without a session requirement, with a 15 s deadline (`:374`).

| Producer of code `paused`                                                 | Source                                                                         | Can it answer `apps.list`?                                       |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `WindowService::list`                                                     | `windows_apps.cpp:9`, `:43-84`: only `failed`, `unavailable` or `changed`      | no                                                               |
| Session policy pause                                                      | `windows_policy.cpp:331` exempts observe and capture; `:343` is launch only    | no: titles go through `disclose`, the observe check (`:345-347`) |
| Admission refusal                                                         | `server.cpp:599-602`                                                           | no: `paused` only for mutating registrations                     |
| Warmup expiry                                                             | `server.cpp:272-300`, `:585-588`                                               | no: only mutating registrations wait (`:273`)                    |
| Queue cancellation at a pause                                             | `cancel_queue(true, L"paused")` at `server.cpp:716` and `:746`                 | no: only mutating queued requests (`:454`)                       |
| Physical takeover, "Physical input paused the task."                      | `Dispatcher::physical_input`, `server.cpp:709-720`, `cancel_active` at `:716`  | yes, any active request; excluded by the row evidence below      |
| First native detection loss, "Native input detection became unavailable." | `Dispatcher::input_detection`, `server.cpp:730-748`, `cancel_active` at `:746` | yes, any active request; the identified mechanism                |

No other Windows source emits code `paused` (the remaining `L"paused"` literals in `server.cpp` and `capture.cpp:280` are argument and reply field names).

**Hypotheses.** (a) is rejected: a process that exits or refuses an identity query during enumeration makes `list` fail with `changed`, `unavailable` or `failed`, never `paused`, so no enumeration change is needed. (b) is rejected: a target window that is not yet listed gives `ok:true` and fails later at `apps-list-target` (`privileged_self_test.cpp:778`), not at `apps-list-reply`. In runs 1 and 3, row 2's `apps.list {"installed":true}` ran after the pause, returned `ok` and listed the running target, or row 2 would have failed at `installed-list-reply` instead of skipping `target_not_installed`.

**Physical takeover is excluded by the rows, not from code.** A takeover's `user_input` event sets `ScenarioState::physical` (`privileged_self_test.cpp:269`), and `await_input` (`:467-475`, called at `:792`) would then throw `physical_takeover`, skipping every unrun row. In runs 1 and 3, rows 3, 4, 15 and 16 ran and passed instead, and rows 5 to 12 skipped `input_detection_unavailable`. This strongly supports the detection loss, but it is not proven from code alone: the event is published only after a confirmed owned-input cleanup and only while its authority is current (`server.cpp:357`, `:423-426`). In the HF-5 runs the settle pauses the session before row 1, so neither pause can cancel row 1 there. A detection loss in the paused phase cancels nothing (`server.cpp:742`). Physical input in a paused session is not a takeover (`user_input.cpp:314`) and only publishes a `user_input` event, which `await_input` turns into `physical_takeover` skips from row 3. A takeover therefore shows in those runs as `physical_takeover` skips, which the stop rules below treat as non-qualifying. The new `authority-paused-*` stages name a pause cancellation wherever one still happens; none is expected once the settle has paused the session.

**Mechanism.** Since W9 the isolated self-test never calibrates before row 16 (`setup_start` appears only at `privileged_self_test.cpp:926`). `UserInputService::session_changed` therefore revokes the begun session's scope at creation (`windows/src/user_input.cpp:421`), and `ready_locked` can never return ready (`:215`). The scope's only possible verdict is "unavailable", published once, when `fail_locked` first runs for it (`:243-247`, `detection_locked` at `:237-242`). `fail_locked` runs on environment-dependent triggers: an environment that is not ready or was observed more than 100 ms earlier (`user_input_native.cpp:71`, which also invalidates the context at `:159`), a configuration or unknown-origin sample (`user_input.cpp:279`), or a listener or context mismatch at the 100 ms cycle (`:339-342`). The first loss reaches `Dispatcher::input_detection`, which pauses the session, rotates the generation and cancels whatever request is active (`server.cpp:742-746`); a repeated loss in the paused phase does none of that (`:742`). So if the verdict lands while row 1's `apps.list` is in flight, that request fails `paused`; if it lands earlier, later or never, row 1 passes. That fits two failures in three x64 runs at `289af6bb`, the ARM64 failure, and the pass/fail changes across `a0e5ce8e`, `f830e3fd`, `0ee486e3` and `cf2373c4` on identical native source. Which trigger fires on hosted runners is not identified and is not needed for the fix; HF-5 claims none.

**Fix, in the isolated self-test only.**

- `NativeScenario::settle_input_detection` (`privileged_self_test.cpp:482-503`) returns at once when the detector did not start (no listener, so no verdict can race) or when the session is already paused. Otherwise it forces its only possible verdict through the existing public `UserInputService::setup_reset` (`user_input.cpp:410-413`), which, uncalibrated, only revokes the current scope and defers one `probe_missing` notice to the next 100 ms tick (`:365`). It then polls every 10 ms, for at most 1,000 ms, until the session is paused. Any pause settles it, whatever caused it: it never skips, and a physical takeover during the settle pauses the session, ends the settle, and leaves rows 1 and 2 to run exactly as they did before HF-5. It sends no Dispatcher request and retries nothing.
- A missing pause fails closed with reason `deadline_exceeded` and one of three new closed stages: `input-settle-context` (run authority, the Dispatcher session or the context was lost), `input-settle-session` (the session epoch changed without a pause, so `UserInputService::deliver` dropped the deferred notice, `user_input.cpp:222`), or `input-settle-verdict` (no verdict by the deadline). The pure `windows_privileged_settle_stage` (`privileged_self_test.cpp:613-621`, declared at `privileged_self_test.h:120-122`, which now includes `server.h` for `ServerSession`) chooses the stage after the deadline. It checks the context first and the pause before the scope comparison, because the pause itself rotates the observation epoch.
- It is called exactly once (`:775`), after `report.checks[0]` is set to passed (`:774`) and before `run(1, …)` (`:776`). The takeover skip policy is that of `272ccb6d`: `await_input` (`:792`) stays the only point that skips the unrun rows for a takeover, and `require_input` and row 16 keep their own per-row skips. A settle failure overwrites row 0 through the outer catch, which now keeps the failure's closed stage and native code (`:942`), exactly as the per-row `run` handler does (`:744`).
- `windows_privileged_origin_stage` maps the two Dispatcher pause cancellations to `authority-paused-detection` and `authority-paused-physical` (`:625-627`), before the capture-trace checks, like `authority-completion`. The admission refusal "Fresh native input detection was not confirmed before admission." keeps its fallback stage.
- The stage catalog grows from 219 to 224 (`privileged_self_test.h:25`, the five literals at `:95-96`), appended in the same order to `WINDOWS_NATIVE_STAGES` (`packages/desktop/scripts/computer-helper-selftest-windows.ts:312-316`). The report decoder and the `computer-helper.yml` and `test.yml` summaries read that list; no workflow file changes.
- Unchanged: the Dispatcher, `WindowService`, `UserInputService`, every predicate, deadline, retry, identity, target, authority and skip policy, and `run(1)` itself (one `apps.list`, no wait). The shipped Windows helper bytes do change, because `privileged_self_test.cpp` is compiled into the helper's core library (`windows/CMakeLists.txt:86`), but only on the `--self-test privileged` path (`windows/src/core.cpp:513-516`), which reaches the scenario only with `VECTOR_COMPUTER_TEST_ISOLATED=1` and a verified target (`privileged_self_test.cpp:761-764`).

**What the fix changes in outcomes.** Rows 1 to 4 and 15 now always run in a paused phase, the detection-paused one unless a takeover paused the session first, which is what already happened whenever the verdict landed before row 1. A paused session may observe and capture (D18 item 3, [hands-deviations.md](./hands-deviations.md#d18--task-controller-phase-fencing-and-lifecycle); `windows_policy.cpp:331`), non-mutating work is admitted while paused (`server.cpp:599-602`), and a repeated loss neither cancels nor rotates. Rows 5 to 12 have skipped `input_detection_unavailable` on every host since W9, and row 16 uses its own setup scope. The isolated scenario therefore no longer runs a session-bound request in an unpaused phase. The product never observes or acts in the foreground before `inputAlive === true` (D18 item 2; `beforeStep`, `packages/desktop/src/main/computer-session.ts:693`, check at `:749-755`), and the unpaused Dispatcher paths stay covered by the pure server tests.

**Tests.**

- `windows/tests/server_tests.cpp:639-657`: an in-flight non-mutating `apps.list` (registered as at `:374`) fails `paused` with "Native input detection became unavailable." at the first loss, its late completion is inert, a paused-phase `apps.list` is admitted, a repeated loss (`secure_desktop`) neither cancels it nor rotates the observation epoch, and it completes `ok`. `:658-671` does the same for a physical takeover ("Physical input paused the task."), and a detection loss after the takeover cancels nothing.
- `windows/tests/user_input_tests.cpp:147-159`: an uncalibrated scope publishes nothing for 1,000 ms without a trigger, then exactly one `probe_missing` verdict for the current session when the environment becomes unavailable, and no second one. After `setup_reset`, the uncalibrated scope publishes nothing until the next tick, then exactly one `probe_missing` verdict carrying the session's epochs, arms no setup or health and never becomes foreground-ready. A scope replaced before the tick drops the deferred verdict.
- `windows/tests/privileged_self_test_tests.cpp:165-198`: no stage slot is empty; both pause messages map to their own stage with the `apps-list-reply` fallback and with a `capture-native-read` trace; a row with either stage and nativeCode `paused` is valid, exits 1 and carries no message text; the admission refusal keeps its fallback; and each settle stage makes a valid staged row-0 failure.
- `windows/tests/privileged_self_test_tests.cpp:199-222`, the pure `windows_privileged_settle_stage`: a paused session whose observation epoch rotated is settled; a lost context gives `input-settle-context` before every other outcome, paused or not, and so does a missing session; an unpaused, unchanged scope gives `input-settle-verdict`; a change of the id, run epoch, observation epoch or revision (set or cleared) gives `input-settle-session`, and the same change while paused is settled; and every outcome is a closed stage.
- `packages/desktop/scripts/computer-helper-selftest.test.ts:408`, "Windows isolated scenario settles the uncalibrated detection verdict before the first session-bound request", as reduced in review round two (below), keeps only what no native group can check: the declared stage-array size equals `WINDOWS_NATIVE_STAGES.length`; `settle_input_detection()` appears only in its definition and one call; that call comes after row 0 is marked passed and before `run(1, `; the settle body contains no `ScenarioSkip`, `request(`, `begin(` or `receive(`; and the outer `ScenarioFailure` catch keeps the failure's stage and native code on row 0, which no native group exercises because none runs the integrated runner. The existing parity test covers the five new stages. The post-deadline classification is exercised natively by `privileged_self_test_tests.cpp:199-222`, and the two pause cancellations by `server_tests.cpp:639-671`.

Rules recorded in advance:

- A compile failure or a failed native-units group is not a qualifying run. It is recorded, fixed in its own commit, and then the two sequential qualifying runs are dispatched.
- No rerun, except for an exact listed known flake.
- For each run: run, attempt and UTC times, job ids and conclusions, the native-units step, the core and isolated count lines, every failed and skipped row with its stage and nativeCode, `apps-running-target` on x64 and ARM64, the staged Stop line, and report artifact ids, with archives "not downloaded" unless downloaded and rehashed. Each ARM64 row is set beside its `289af6bb` result for HF-6, with no new attribution.
- Stop and report to the lead, without a third dispatch, if row 0 fails at any `input-settle-*` stage (recorded word for word; its cause is not concluded from the stage alone); if `apps-running-target` fails at `authority-paused-*` or at any other stage (recorded word for word as "cause still unidentified" for HF-8); if an x64 row that passed 3/3 at `289af6bb` now fails (`privileged-isolated-target`, `capture-color-patch`, `capture-affinity-exclusion`, `capture-isolated-window-worker`, `input-listener-health` or `isolated-target-cleanup`), as a possible regression from the phase change; or if the ARM64 rows are not reached because windows-build failed elsewhere (for example the open `observe-up` Stop) or the required ARM64 core step failed. In that last case the report says "x64 confirmed, ARM64 not reached", and the lead decides whether one more dispatch is allowed only for an unreached ARM64 job.
- Stop and report to the lead, without a third dispatch, if any HF-5 run's isolated report skips any row as `physical_takeover`. Record the rows exactly as reported and treat the run as non-qualifying. A takeover is the cause HF-5 has not yet excluded from code, and on this route (`privileged-required: false`) the isolated step can still conclude success. Since the review the settle never skips, so `apps-running-target` cannot itself skip `physical_takeover`. A takeover during the settle's wait pauses the session and ends the settle. Once the session is paused, physical input is not a takeover and cancels nothing (`user_input.cpp:314`); it publishes a `user_input` event, and `await_input` then skips the unrun rows from row 3.
- The evidence file is named by the Pacific date of the first HF-5 run's `created_at` (`hands-windows-validation-<date>.json`, with the 11-14 file's top-level keys plus `rootCause`, `fix`, `hf5Runs`, `decisions` and `pending`). The 11-14 file is the one exception to this rule: it kept the HF-4 brief's name, while its `datePacific` is the 15 November date of its first dispatch (HF-4). `parentRevision` and `testedRevision` are the head the runs were dispatched at, which is the fix commit unless a compile-fix commit follows; `startRevision` is `272ccb6d`. The 11-14 file stays unchanged.
- HF-5 is done only when both runs pass `apps-running-target` on x64 and ARM64 and that is recorded here and in the evidence file.

**Deferred.**

- The two dispatches, the evidence file, and the post-run updates to [the current state](./hands-current-state.md), [the handoff](./hands-handoff.md) line 3 and [the diagnostic note](./hands-uia-timeout-diagnostic.md). The current-state page is updated with the run results, not before them; it is at its 60-line limit. Done in review round two, except that run 1 did not reach ARM64, which leaves one ARM64-only dispatch to the lead's decision.
- The C++ groups, which compile and run only in CI (windows-build "native units" and the ARM64 unit step).
- The hosted-runner trigger of the detection loss.
- The Windows begin-barrier observation (D19 item 8), recorded in the deviation entry for the lead. 21 Nov 2026: option B (W9 meets the intent through `beforeStep`) is proposed for the lead in [D19 item 8 — begin barrier](./hands-deviations.md#d19-item-8--begin-barrier-option-b-proposed-for-the-lead-21-november-2026).
- With HF-6 and the lead: ARM64 `capture-color-patch`, `capture-affinity-exclusion` and `input-listener-health`, the run 3 ARM64 core `unit` row, and the `observe-up` Stop. The `cf2373c4` x64 capture failures stay on record unattributed.
- Mac lock confirmation at the merged source, packaging (the owner's preview-ref decision), HF-2's engine-shard line, and every owner gate in the [current state](./hands-current-state.md#owner-gates).

HF-5 gives no release, signing, device or qualification credit.

## HF-6 — ARM64 listener-health and affinity rows: cause still unidentified (16 November 2026)

HF-6 looked for the cause of the two ARM64 rows that HF-4 handed to it: `privileged.capture-affinity-exclusion` and `privileged.input-listener-health`. It started at `ef9f809d9e86ec332a9ae837c54a29d475917d34`, which equalled `origin/hands-finish`. At write time (about 09:30 UTC on 16 November), `origin/main` was `b362fdf6`, the PR #33 merge commit (parents `58f85edc` and `67ec3a60`, committed at 09:23Z), and `origin/release-finish` was `67ec3a60`. Merging `main` into this branch is the lead's step, so HF-6 merged nothing. The newest `test.yml` run on `hands-finish` was `43816713717` at `289af6bb`; no run existed at `ef9f809d`. Under its brief, a row whose printed stage does not identify a cause ends with "cause still unidentified", no code change and no fix commit. Both rows end that way. HF-6 changed no code, test or workflow and dispatched nothing. Its commit gate committed this record, its deviation entry and the note correction as one docs-only commit, titled `docs(hands)` instead of the gate's `fix(hands): resolve …` title, because no row was resolved. [Deviation entry](./hands-deviations.md#hf-6--arm64-listener-health-and-affinity-the-printed-stages-do-not-identify-a-cause).

**Source scope.** Line numbers are at `ef9f809d`; the failing run was at `289af6bb`. `user_input.cpp`, `user_input.h`, `user_input_native.cpp`, `capture.cpp`, `availability.cpp`, `server.cpp` and `privileged_target.cpp` are identical at both heads. `privileged_self_test.cpp` differs only by HF-5's settle, its stage function, two origin mappings and the outer catch, so the code of rows 3, 4 and 16 is the same at both. The state they start from is not: at `ef9f809d`, HF-5's settle (`settle_input_detection`, `:489-503`, called at `:775`) calls `setup_reset()` before row 1, unless the session is already paused, and waits for the pause, so rows 3, 4 and 16 start from a different detector and session state than at `289af6bb`.

**Gate.** HF-4's evidence file states the conditions (`decisionGates.hf6Affinity` and `decisionGates.hf6Listener` in [hands-windows-validation-2026-11-14.json](./evidence/hands-windows-validation-2026-11-14.json)). ARM64 job `133112685091` (run [43809140577](https://github.com/scr1pter/claudeondfsfdaf/actions/runs/43809140577) attempt 1, head `289af6bb`; workflow conclusion `success` because the isolated step is `continue-on-error` when `privileged-required` is false, `computer-helper.yml:956`) printed the two rows below at 05:51:05Z. The isolated report archive was not downloaded; the rows were read from the job log.

- `FAILED privileged.capture-affinity-exclusion: native_operation_failed [stage=capture-service, nativeCode=stale_frame]`. `capture-service` is not one of the 13 `affinity-plan-*` stages, so the affinity condition is not met.
- `FAILED privileged.input-listener-health: assertion_failed [stage=input-health-ready, nativeCode=unknown]`. `input-health-ready` is one of the three listener stages, so the listener condition is met and HF-6 analysed the row.

Only HF-4's stages gate HF-6. HF-5's two runs at `ef9f809d` will print fresh ARM64 row 4 and row 16 stages. HF-5's settle pauses the session before row 1, so at `ef9f809d` both row 4 and row 16 run after that pause and either stage may differ; the lead may re-gate HF-6 on both rows from those runs. HF-6 records none of HF-5's run data.

**Affinity row: what `capture-service` / `stale_frame` can mean.** Only `windows_privileged_origin_stage` returns `capture-service` (`privileged_self_test.cpp:634`), and within row 4 only `NativeScenario::success` (`:457-463`) calls it, for a reply with `ok:false`; the other caller, `:887`, is row 15's forced-worker read. The stage therefore means that a `capture` reply carried the default message of `capture.cpp:12`, "The native capture changed. Take a new screenshot.", and no capture trace, inventory or read stage. The reason `native_operation_failed` is the one `success` always throws (`:460`), whatever the native code.

- Row 4 (`:797-820`) sends two capture replies through `success`. The first is its window capture in `capture()` (`window-capture-reply`, `:540-542`, called at `:801`). The second is its display capture (`display-capture-reply`, `:802-804`), which runs only after the 13 plan stages in `capture_affinity_display` (`:417-456`). The printed stage does not say which one failed. A window-capture failure means no plan stage ran, and a display-capture failure means all 13 passed.
- Inside `CaptureService::capture`, four sites raise `stale_frame` with the default message. `capture.cpp:53` checks a surface's window identity in the privacy plan. `:296` checks a zoom source, which the self-test never sends. `:310` fails when the inventory read again after the native read differs from the first, or when the context is no longer current. `:330` fails when the receipt registry refuses the record.
- The other `stale_frame` paths in row 4 cannot print `capture-service`. The receipt lookups (`window-capture-receipt`, `:543-545`, and `display-capture-receipt`, `:805`) throw inside `diagnostic_stage`, which prints its own stage (`:30-42`). `CaptureService::current` (`capture.cpp:238-246`), used by `affinity-plan-current-frame` (`:426`) and `affinity-fixture-current` (`:807-811`), returns false instead, which prints `assertion_failed` at that stage.
- In the same ARM64 job, row 3 (`capture-color-patch`, `:796`) failed in its own window capture at `capture-window-worker-read` / `capture_unavailable`, a read-trace stage. Row 4's failing reply carried no trace stage and a different code, so if it was row 4's window capture, it did not fail the way row 3's did. Both rows failed in a capture reply. That is consistent with a shared ARM64 capture problem, but it does not show one.
- The brief's background, "failed at `affinity-sample-plan`, before any display read or native sample", describes `503418a1`'s coarse stage and does not carry over to `289af6bb`.
- HF-6 changes nothing for this row. `capture-color-patch` stays with the lead.

**Listener row: what `input-health-ready` means.** Row 16 (`:906-941`) works in this order:

1. It arms a `ResetSetup` guard (`:910`).
2. It checks authority and target (`:911-912`).
3. It takes one detector snapshot (`:913`) and checks the start outcome and listener status from it (`:914-924`).
4. It starts setup (`input-listener-setup`, `:925-927`).
5. The stage `input-listener-health` (`:928-934`) polls every 5 ms, for at most 750 ms, while authority and target are current and health is false (`:929-931`).
6. It then checks context, target and health, in that order (`:932-934`).

`input-health-ready` therefore means authority and target were current at `:932-933`, and `UserInputService::listener_health_ready()` (`user_input.cpp:398`) returned false at the re-check on `:934`. The loop also ends early when a poll sees health true, so health may have been true briefly before it turned false. After `setup_start`, the selected scope is the new setup scope (`user_input.cpp:196`, `:400-409`). `health_ready_locked` (`:201-205`) requires seven terms together:

1. the scope is active (`:195`);
2. the cached listener status is healthy (`:19`);
3. the scope's listener generation and context equal the cached ones;
4. the scope is neither failed nor revoked;
5. it has a pulse less than 2,500 ms old, which only an accepted health receipt sets (`:264`);
6. its environment is fresh: ready, in the scope's context, and observed less than 500 ms earlier (`:198-200`);
7. no challenge for the scope is outside its window.

Terms 3 and 4 never recover inside the row. A failed setup scope is replaced only by a new `setup_start` or a `setup_reset`, so a single trigger after setup (or the one before it in the P1 sub-case below) fails the row.

**Producers that can leave a term false within 750 ms.** Lines are in `user_input.cpp` unless the row names `user_input_native.cpp`.

| Producer                                       | Source                                                 | Effect                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ---------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| P1 cycle refusal                               | `:339-342`                                             | `fail_locked`, which fails the scope (term 4) and, after a context bump, term 3. Its triggers are an unhealthy native status; an environment that is not ready, is stale or belongs to another context; and a calibration or scope listener/context mismatch. The native `environment()` bumps the context whenever its observation is not ready (`user_input_native.cpp:156-162`). `observe_environment` (`user_input_native.cpp:51-74`) is not ready in these cases: the helper token or desktop is not permissible (`:54-56`); there is no foreground window, thread or process id (`:57-59`); the foreground process cannot be opened or has exited (`:60-61`); the foreground changes or exits during the observation (`:63-65`); the foreground token is elevated or belongs to another session or logon (`:66`, `availability.cpp:31-41`); the input desktop is not the default one on a visible station (`:68-69`, `availability.cpp:25-27`); or the observation took over 100 ms (`:70-71`). Any permissible foreground window is ready; it need not be the fixture. |
| P1 sub-case: a stale context captured at setup | `:334`, `:337`, `:406`; `make_scope_locked` `:206-212` | `cycle` reads `status()` before `environment()` (`:334`), so after a not-ready observation the cached listener context (`:337`) lags the native one by one. `setup_start` copies that cached context into the calibration (`:406`) and into the new scope (`:209`). If the last cycle before `setup_start` was not ready, the next cycle finds a context mismatch and fails the scope (`:340-342`), however ready the environment is by then.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| P2 tick refusal                                | `:366-368`                                             | `fail_locked` while calibrating, when the cached environment is not fresh or the open challenge has left its window. If the cached environment is not ready, or is over 500 ms old, when `setup_start` runs, the next tick fails the new scope within 100 ms.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| P3 challenge expiry                            | `:324-330`                                             | No accepted receipt within the challenge's 500 ms. The listener answers only after `pump_messages` leaves no backlog (`user_input_native.cpp:261-270`, `:353-356`). `process_health` repeats the route and environment checks inside the same deadline (`user_input_native.cpp:271-296`), and its `environment()` call (`:280`) bumps the context as in P1.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| P4 receipt refusal                             | `:259-262`                                             | `fail_locked` on a receipt that is unhealthy (for example after a not-ready environment in `process_health`) or late.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| P5 raw samples                                 | `:271-279`                                             | `fail_locked` on a sample from another listener or a newer context, on a configuration sample, or on an unknown-origin sample. A device change (`user_input_native.cpp:242`), a malformed packet (`:184-219`) or an unknown origin (`:227`) also bumps the native context.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| P6 timer or submission failure                 | `:355`, `:359`                                         | `fail_locked` when the challenge timer cannot be scheduled or the listener submission fails.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| P7 no challenge in time                        | `:343-347`, `:370`                                     | No `fail_locked`: a challenge needs no open challenge for the scope, and a new cycle starts only when the observation worker is free, so term 5 stays false.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| P8 listener failure                            | `user_input_native.cpp:279`, `:282`, `:350-358`        | Route loss or listener-thread failure, which makes terms 2 and 3 false.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

**Evidence.**

- x64 passed row 16 in every recorded run: at `a0e5ce8e` (run 43560683817, "explicit listener health"), at `503418a1` (job `132571855706`), at `f830e3fd`, `0ee486e3` and `cf2373c4` (not among those jobs' failed or skipped rows), and in all three HF-4 runs. It runs on `windows-2025` (`computer-helper.yml:613`).
- ARM64 failed row 16 in both runs that reached it, on identical listener and capture source: every file under `windows/src` except `privileged_self_test.cpp`/`.h`, including `user_input*.cpp`/`.h`, `capture.cpp` and `availability.cpp`, is unchanged between the two heads. `privileged_self_test.cpp`/`.h` differ only by `a0fbfa3b`'s stage instrumentation, `privileged_self_test_tests.cpp` also by `289af6bb`'s one-line compile fix, and `identity.json` only by the version label (`1.0.16` at `503418a1`, `1.0.15` at `289af6bb`). The two runs were `503418a1` job `132576158733` at the coarse stage `input-listener-health`, and `289af6bb` job `133112685091` at `input-health-ready`. It runs on `windows-11-arm` (`:876`). Job `133112685091`'s log records the image `windows-11-vs2026-arm64` `20261112.217.1` and Microsoft Windows 11 Enterprise `10.0.26200`.
- The x64 and ARM64 jobs use the same isolated invocation with `VECTOR_COMPUTER_TEST_ISOLATED=1` (`:714-723` and `:950-961`).
- An early trigger is not specific to ARM64. Under HF-5's attribution (most likely the first native-detection loss), `apps-list-reply` / `paused` shows that a `fail_locked` trigger fired during row 1. That happened on x64 in HF-4 runs 1 and 3, as well as on ARM64 in run 1. x64 still passed row 16 in those runs, so no trigger fell inside its row 16 window. On ARM64 a trigger persisted into row 16's window or recurred inside it, or the receipt path was too slow (P3, P7). The evidence points to persistence or recurrence on ARM64, not to whether a trigger occurs at all.

**Hypotheses, none excluded.** The stage does not choose among P1 to P8, and no log line, report field or code path at `ef9f809d` records which producer fired.

- H-FG (P1, foreground or desktop): there is no foreground window; the foreground changes or exits during the observation; the foreground token is elevated or belongs to another session or logon; or the input desktop is not the default. A foreground window that is not the fixture is not itself a refusal. The fixture never makes itself foreground (`privileged_target.cpp:328` only shows it), and the self-test's only focus request (`window.focus`, `privileged_self_test.cpp:792-795`) runs only when `await_input()` succeeds, which needs `foreground_ready` and so never happens in this uncalibrated session. Whichever window is foreground on the runner therefore decides these branches. Which window that was on ARM64 is recorded nowhere. Not excluded.
- H-budget (P1, the 100 ms observation budget): `observe_environment` reads the helper token, the desktop and the foreground token. On a slower runner it could run past 100 ms. Not excluded.
- H-raw (P5): device-change, malformed or unknown-origin samples bump the context. Not excluded.
- H-latency (P3, P7): the challenge or its receipt does not complete within the fixed budgets. Not excluded.
- H-listener (P8): route loss or listener-thread failure after the pre-setup snapshot. Not excluded.

**Why no change.** The brief allows two kinds of fix, each for an identified cause. A fixture or harness cause is fixed in the self-test, without weakening any predicate, the single session snapshot, the context checks or the original search order. A shipping cause is fixed with a pure native regression and a deviation entry. Neither cause is identified, so any change would be a guess. The changes that could make row 16 pass without a cause are all forbidden: a longer 750 ms deadline, a longer 500 ms challenge or freshness window, a larger 100 ms observation budget, a weaker predicate, a retry, a calibration shortcut, input authority, or turning the failure into a skip.

- The brief forbids changing the deadline, the calibration requirement or input authority. Listener health never grants input ([W9](./hands-deviations.md#w9--raw-input-evidence-exact-readiness-and-final-revocation), `hands-deviations.md:778`; owner approval at `:1278`).
- W9 proposal item 6 (`hands-deviations.md:1293`) requires that any unknown sample, device change or desktop, session or integrity change revokes readiness, that no heartbeat revives it, and that state which cannot be verified stays paused.
- Row 16's `ResetSetup` guard, its setup gate and `input-calibration-required` (`:936-940`) are unchanged.

- "ARM64 privileged.capture-affinity-exclusion: HF-4 printed capture-service / stale_frame (run 43809140577, job 133112685091), outside HF-6's affinity-plan-\* condition; it does not show whether the plan stages ran. Cause still unidentified."
- "ARM64 privileged.input-listener-health: HF-4 printed input-health-ready / unknown (same job); listener_health_ready() was false at the re-check after the unchanged 750 ms wait, with authority and target current; the stage does not select among the HF-6 producers P1 to P8. Cause still unidentified."

**Proposed next step for the lead.** A separate diagnostic package, with its own brief and done condition, could narrow the listener cause. It would record closed, read-only facts at the `input-health-ready` failure, taken synchronously in the self-test, that map to the refusal branches above. The facts are the setup state, the listener failure code and context change since the pre-setup snapshot, whether the foreground window is null, and the closed surface state. It is described, with its limits and outcome table, in point 6 of the [deviation entry](./hands-deviations.md#hf-6--arm64-listener-health-and-affinity-the-printed-stages-do-not-identify-a-cause). HF-6 did not build it.
